     Nomination Policy and Procedure

     The Company has adopted policies and procedures for the nomination and election of Directors (the “Nomination
     Policy”). The Nomination Policy sets out the criteria and procedures for election and performance evaluation, and
     provides guidance to the Board on the nomination and appointment of Directors. The Board believes that a clear election
     process facilitates good corporate governance, ensures the continuity of the Board, maintains the Board’s leadership,
     and enhances the efficiency and diversity of the Board. Both the Nomination Committee and the Board may nominate
     candidates for directorships. In assessing the suitability of recommended candidates and their potential contribution to
     the Board, the Nomination Committee may refer to several selection criteria such as integrity, professional qualifications
     and skills, knowledge, experience, commitment and relevant contribution. The Nomination Committee is required to report
     the assessment results and provide relevant recommendations to the Board on the appointment of suitable candidates
     for Directors, enabling the Board to make decisions and formulate the overall election plan, which will be submitted to the
     shareholders’ meeting for shareholders’ approval.

     The Board and the Nomination Committee intend to review the nomination policy at least annually and expect that
     necessary amendments may be made from time to time in response to the evolving needs and circumstances of the
     Company and where applicable statutory obligations or requirements under the Listing Rules, Hong Kong laws or Chinese
     laws or other regulatory changes.

     Board Diversity Policy

     We have adopted the board diversity policy which sets out the objective and approach for achieving and maintaining the
     diversity of the Board in order to enhance its effectiveness. In accordance with the board diversity policy, the Company
     seeks to achieve board diversity by taking into account a number of factors, including but not limited to gender, age,
     cultural and educational background, professional experience, skills, knowledge and/or length of service. The ultimate
     selection of Board candidates will be based on merit and potential contribution to our Board having due regard to the
     benefits of diversity on the Board and also the specific needs of the Company without focusing on a single diversity aspect.
     The Company’s Directors have a balanced mix of knowledge and skills, including overall management and strategic
     development as well as knowledge and experience in areas such as investment and operations management. They
     obtained degrees in various areas including engineering mechanics, MBA, journalism and communication and accounting.
     Furthermore, our Board has a diverse age and gender representation. Our Board currently comprises one female Director
     and nine male Directors, ranging from 40 years old to 62 years old.
                                                                     2025 ANNUAL REPORT      BRETON TECHNOLOGY CO., LTD.        45




7. CORPORATE GOVERNANCE REPORT


With regards to gender diversity on the Board, the Company recognizes the particular importance of gender diversity. The
Company has taken and will continue to take steps to promote and enhance gender diversity at all levels of the Company,
including but without limitation at our Board and senior management levels. The Company will maintain a focus on gender
diversity when recruiting staff at the mid to senior level so as to develop a pipeline of potential female successors to the
Board. The Group will also identify and select several female individuals with a diverse range of skills, experience and
knowledge in different fields from time to time, and maintain a list of such female individuals who possess qualities to
become our Board members, which will be reviewed by the nomination committee periodically to maintain gender diversity
of the Board.

The Nomination Committee will discuss and agree on expected goals at least once a year to ensure board diversity, and
review and, where necessary, update the board diversity policy to ensure that the policy remains effective. The Company
will disclose the biographical details of each Director and report on the implementation of the board diversity policy in its
annual corporate governance report.

Based on the review conducted by the Nomination Committee for the year ended December 31, 2025, taking into account
the Company’s existing business model, specific needs, and the diverse backgrounds of the Directors, the Nomination
Committee and the Board consider that the current composition of the Board is consistent with the Company’s Board
Diversity Policy. No measurable targets have been set by the Board.

Gender Diversity

The Company values gender diversity at all levels of the Group. As of December 31, 2025, out of the Group’s 346
employees, there were 294 male employees and 52 female employees, representing approximately 84.97% and 15.03%
respectively of the total workforce (including senior management). The Group has taken gender diversity into account in its
recruitment process and is committed to increasing the proportion of female employees wherever possible in the future. To
achieve gender diversity among employees, the Company is committed to creating favourable conditions in the workplace
to continuously attract employees of different genders to join the Group. In this process, the Company may face challenges
in matching talents of a particular gender in the human resources market with the education, experience and skills required
for positions within the Group. Notwithstanding the challenges, the Company remains committed to maintaining a gender-
balanced workforce.

Chartered Governance Institute and The Chartered Governance Institute in the United Kingdom, and a member of the
executive committee of the Mexican Chamber of Commerce in Hong Kong.

For the year ended December 31, 2025, Ms. Shum Kit Han and Mr. Liu Xingyu have each received not less than 15 hours
of relevant professional training in compliance with Rule 3.29 of the Listing Rules.

Mr. Liu Xingyu has resigned as the Joint Company Secretary of the Company due to a work change, with effect from

Shareholders’ Rights
Convening an Extraordinary Shareholders’ Meeting

According to Article 79 of the Articles of Association, the shareholders who individually or jointly hold more than 10% of
the voting shares of the Company shall have the right to propose to the Board of Directors to convene an extraordinary
shareholders’ meeting or a class shareholders’ meeting, and shall make such request to the Board of Directors in writing.
The Board shall, in accordance with the provisions of laws, administrative laws and regulations, the listing rules of the
stock exchange where the Company is listed and the Articles of Association of the Company, provide written feedback
on whether or not to agree to hold an extraordinary shareholders’ meeting or a class general meeting within 10 days after
receiving the request.
48   BRETON TECHNOLOGY CO., LTD.        2025 ANNUAL REPORT




     7. CORPORATE GOVERNANCE REPORT


     If the Board agrees to convene an extraordinary shareholders’ meeting or a class shareholders’ meeting, a notice of the
     meeting shall be issued within 5 days after the resolution of the Board is passed. Changes made to the original request in
     the notice shall be approved by relevant shareholders. If the Board does not agree to hold the extraordinary shareholders’
     meeting or the class shareholders’ meeting or fails to give a reply within 10 days after receipt of the proposal,
     shareholder(s) severally or jointly holding no less than 10% voting shares of the Company shall be entitled to propose to
     convene an extraordinary shareholders’ or a class shareholders’ meeting, and shall make such request in writing to the
     Audit Committee.

     If the Audit Committee agrees to convene the extraordinary shareholders’ meeting or the class shareholders’ meeting,
     it shall serve a notice of such meeting within 5 days after receipt of the said request. In the event of any change to the
     original proposal, the consent of relevant shareholder(s) shall be obtained. If the Audit Committee fails to issue the notice
     for the shareholders’ meeting or the class shareholders’ meeting within the specified time, the shareholder(s) severally or
     jointly holding no less than 10% voting shares of the Company for no less than 90 consecutive days may convene and
     preside over such meeting on his or their own.

     Proposals Made at the Shareholders’ Meeting

     According to Article 84 of the Articles of Association, the shareholders who individually or collectively hold more than 1%
     of the Company’s shares may put forward an interim proposal and submit it to the convener in writing 10 working days
     before the shareholders’ meeting. The convener shall, within 2 days after receipt of the proposal, issue a supplementary
     notice of the shareholders’ meeting, informing the content of the interim proposal; provided that this shall not apply where
     such proposal violates any laws, administrative regulations, the listing rules of the stock exchange where the Company
     is listed or the provisions of the Articles of Association, or does not fall within the scope of authority of the shareholders’
     meeting.

     Enquiries to the Board

     Shareholders may request the Board of Directors for enquiries. For the avoidance of doubt, shareholders shall deposit
     and send the original copy of the duly signed written request, notice or statement or enquiry (as the case may be) to the
     above address, and provide their full name, contact information and proof of identity for the same to be valid. Shareholder
     information may be disclosed in accordance with the law. The Company will not normally deal with oral or anonymous
     inquiries.

     Shareholders may send the above enquiries or requests to the following contact details:

Communication Policy with Shareholders/Investor Relations

The Company considers that effective communication with shareholders is essential for enhancing investor relations and
understanding of the Group’s business performance and strategies. The Company strives to maintain an ongoing dialogue
with its shareholders, particularly through annual shareholders’ meetings and extraordinary shareholders’ meetings. At
the annual shareholders’ meeting, directors (or their proxy, where applicable) are available to meet with shareholders and
respond to their enquiries.

To safeguard shareholders’ interests and rights, each material issue, including the election of individual directors, shall be
proposed as a separate resolution at a shareholders’ meeting. All resolutions put forward at a shareholder meeting shall
be voted on by poll pursuant to the Listing Rules and poll results will be posted on the websites of the Company and the
Stock Exchange after each shareholder meeting.

The Company has established a range of channels to maintain ongoing dialogue with its shareholders, details of which are
set out below:

a)     Shareholders’ Enquiries

       •      Shareholders may access the publicly available information of the Company at any time.

       •      Shareholders may contact the Company’s designated contact persons, email addresses and enquiry hotline
              to raise any question regarding the Company’s matters.

b)     Company’s Correspondence

       •      “Company’s correspondence” shall refer to any document issued or to be issued by the Company for the
              information or action of shareholders, including but not limited to the report of the Board, annual accounts
              and the auditors’ report, interim reports, notices of meetings, circulars and proxy forms. The Company’s
              correspondence dispatched to shareholders is prepared in plain language in both Chinese and English
              to facilitate shareholders’ understanding of the contents. Shareholders shall have the right to choose the
              language (English or Chinese).

       •      Shareholders are encouraged to provide the Company with information such as email addresses to facilitate
              timely and effective communication.

27, 2025, its circular dated September 9, 2025, and its poll results announcement dated September 26, 2025.

Placing, the Board of Directors made corresponding amendments to the Articles of Association, effective from November
24, 2025. For details, please refer to the Company’s announcement dated November 24, 2025.

To further improve the Company’s corporate governance structure, ensure that the Articles of Association remain
consistent with the latest legal and regulatory requirements, while also taking into account the actual needs of the
Company’s business development, the Company convened its first extraordinary shareholders’ meeting of 2026 on
February 5, 2026 at which a resolution to amend the Articles of Association and its appendices was considered and
approved, effective from February 5, 2026, in accordance with the relevant laws, regulations, and normative documents,
including the Company Law, the Guidelines for Articles of Association of Listed Companies (Revised in 2025), the
Listing Rules, as well as regulatory requirements. Upon completion of these amendments, the Company will no longer
have a Supervisory Committee or appoint supervisors. The Audit Committee of the Board of Directors will exercise the
functions and powers of the Supervisory Committee as stipulated in the Company Law, and the Rules of Procedure of the
Supervisory Committee will be correspondingly abolished. For details, please refer to the Company’s announcement dated
January 9, 2026, its circular dated January 16, 2026, and its poll results announcement dated February 5, 2026.

     Dividend Policy

     The Company is a joint stock company incorporated in the People’s Republic of China with limited liability. Whether future
     dividends will be distributed will be determined at the discretion of our Directors and may be based on a variety of factors,
     including our future operations and earnings, capital requirements and surplus, overall financial condition, contractual
     restrictions and other factors our Directors may deem relevant. Any proposed dividend distribution will also be subject to
     the approval of the shareholders’ meeting. The Company does not have a predetermined dividend payout ratio. The current
     regulations in China permit the domestic companies to pay dividends only out of their accumulated distributable after-
     tax profits, after deducting any accumulated loss coverage and appropriations to the statutory and other reserve funds as
     determined in accordance with our Articles of Association and China Accounting Standards and its regulations. Therefore,
     even if we are profitable, we may not have sufficient or any distributable profits to pay dividends to our shareholders. There
     is no assurance that dividends of such amount or any amount may be declared or distributed annually or in any year.

     For the year ended December 31, 2025, the Company did not pay or declare any dividends. The previous dividend
     distribution record of the Company may not be used as a reference or basis for determining the level of dividends that may
     be declared or paid by the Company in the future.

     Risk Management and Internal Control

     The Board deeply understands that it is responsible for the risk management and internal control systems to safeguard
     shareholders’ investments and the Company’s assets, and to review the effectiveness of such systems on an annual basis.
     The Board also clarifies such systems are designed to manage rather than eliminate the risk of failure to achieve business
     objectives, and can only provide reasonable but not absolute assurance against material misstatement or loss.

     The Group has established a comprehensive risk management and internal control governance system with clear levels
     and well-defined responsibilities, comprising three layers: strategic decision-making layer, operational management layer,
     and execution layer.

     The strategic decision-making layer comprises the Board and the Audit Committee. The Board serves as the highest
     decision-making organization for risk management and internal control, responsible for the effectiveness of the Group’s risk
     management and internal control efforts, and hears reports on the management and internal control systems for key risks
     including strategic, operational and ESG risks, as well as their implementation status. The Audit Committee is responsible
     for overseeing risk management and internal control mechanisms, and guiding the Group’s management in implementing
     effective risk management systems.

     The management layer comprises the Risk Management Department and the Internal Audit Department. The Risk
     Management Department is responsible for studying, discussing and organizing the implementation of risk management
     efforts. The Company has established an internal audit department, responsible for reviewing the effectiveness of internal
     controls and reporting any issues identified, thereby improving our internal control systems and procedures through
     the ongoing identification of errors and deficiencies. The internal audit department promptly reports any material issues
     identified to the Audit Committee and the Board of Directors.
                                                                        2025 ANNUAL REPORT        BRETON TECHNOLOGY CO., LTD.         53




7. CORPORATE GOVERNANCE REPORT


The execution layer comprises all business departments. Business departments carry out day-to-day risk management
work in accordance with position requirements, and promptly report to the Internal Audit Department and the Risk Control
Departments any operational and management issue that may affect risks.

The Company’s risk management and internal control systems have been developed with the following principles, features
and processes with defined authority for implementation to promote a compliance culture, to perform independent
review and monitor the risk management progress, to address potential risks related to operations, information security,
intellectual property and anti-corruption, and to provide findings and recommendations for improvement to the Audit
Committee.

Business Operational Risk Management

Business operational risk refers to the risk of direct or indirect financial loss resulting from incomplete or problematic
internal processes, personnel mistakes, IT system failures, or external events. Our sales department, research center,
production and operations department, procurement department, human resources department, public relations
department, legal department, risk management department, finance department, customer service department, quality
department, secretary to the Board’s office, administrative department, IT department, and internal audit department are
collectively responsible for ensuring that our business operations comply with internal procedures. In the event of a major
adverse occurrence, the matter will be escalated to our senior management, and the Board may need to take appropriate
measures.

Anti-Corruption Risk Management

Anti-corruption risk refers to the risk associated with the use of cheating, bribery, or other illegal measures for (i) the pursuit
of improper personal benefits at the expense of our economic interests and (ii) the pursuit of improper interests of our
Company.

The Company has a zero-tolerance policy for corruption and in place the Anti-Corruption Policy to safeguard against
corruption and bribery within the Company. Our internal audit department is directly responsible for anti-corruption
risk management and has established an anti-corruption committee comprising designated personnel from our human
resources, internal audit, and legal departments. We also maintain a whistleblower mechanism to encourage the internal
reporting of suspicious activities. Employees can make anonymous reports to the internal anti-corruption department,
which is responsible for investigating the reported incidents and taking appropriate measures. The Company continues to
carry out anti-corruption and anti-bribery activities to cultivate a culture of integrity and actively organizes anti-corruption
training and inspections to ensure the effectiveness of anti-corruption and anti-bribery. All suppliers are required to sign
anti-corruption commitments before engagement. During the Reporting Period, the Company held 2 anti-corruption
trainings and briefings to all employees. There were no non-compliance cases in relation to bribery and corruption.
54   BRETON TECHNOLOGY CO., LTD.       2025 ANNUAL REPORT




     7. CORPORATE GOVERNANCE REPORT



     Confidentiality Risk Management

     Confidentiality risk arises when an employee breaches their confidentiality obligations by disclosing our or our business
     partners’ trade secrets to third parties. Such actions could harm our commercial interests and expose us to legal actions
     and reputational damage. To address this, we have implemented a stringent confidentiality agreement template that
     employees must sign along with their employment contract before starting their roles.

     This agreement clearly outlines their responsibilities to protect confidential information. We also provide training to raise
     employee awareness of confidentiality, including regular security drills and simulated leak scenarios to enhance vigilance.
     Furthermore, we have enacted a Confidentiality Management Policy that defines the roles and responsibilities of our
     confidentiality team, establishes procedures for managing trade secrets and personnel handling sensitive information,
     monitoring information disclosure and responding to enquiries, and sets forth disciplinary actions and rewards to reinforce
     compliance.

     Business Partner Contract Fraud Risk Management

     Contract fraud risk arises when business partners engage in fraudulent activities, such as forging contracts, providing
     false information, or concealing critical facts, which could mislead us into signing unfavourable agreements and harm our
     interests. To mitigate this risk, we have established a Contract Management Policy that defines procedures for drafting,
     reviewing, and managing contracts. This policy includes a robust review process with multiple approval checkpoints to
     prevent and control contract risks effectively.

     Business Partner Product Quality Risk Management

     Product quality risk arises when products or services provided by business partners have defects or quality issues,
     leading to customer dissatisfaction, harm to our brand image, and a loss of competitive edge. To manage this risk, we
     have implemented the Procurement Management Policy and the Supplier Management Procedure that specify supplier
     management processes and the responsibilities of our relevant departments. Additionally, our quality department annually
     maintains and updates our quality management system documents, including inspection standards and quality control
     procedures, to ensure prompt responses to quality issues. We have also introduced the Quality Issue Accountability
     Policy to define responsibilities for quality problems and ensure accountability in addressing them. The Board reviews
     the effectiveness of the Company’s risk management and internal control systems at least once a year through the Audit
     Committee.
                                                                     2025 ANNUAL REPORT       BRETON TECHNOLOGY CO., LTD.        55




7. CORPORATE GOVERNANCE REPORT


The Company’s risk management and internal control systems have been integrated into business processes as an
inseparable part of the Company’s overall operations. The systems comprise a comprehensive organizational structure
where each position is assigned clear responsibilities and delegated corresponding authorities. The Company has
established a reporting system based on the organizational structure, which includes reporting channels for the heads of
each major business unit to the Board.

The Company has established risk management and internal control systems, as well as an accounting system, to identify
and assess the Company’s risks, formulate risk mitigation strategies, and provide reasonable assurance that assets are
not used or disposed of without authorization, transactions are conducted with management’s authorization, accounting
records can be reliably used for preparing published financial information in the course of business, accountability for
assets and liabilities is maintained, and business operations are conducted in accordance with relevant laws, regulations
and internal guidelines.

The Company has an organizational structure with clear authority and responsibility. Each department is responsible for
its day-to-day operations and is required to report regularly to the executive directors. Each department has established
policies and procedures, which include establishing and maintaining effective policies to ensure that the Company’s risks
are properly identified and appropriate actions are taken to control such risks; establishing a structure with clear authority
and appropriate segregation of duties; monitoring strategic plans and performance, designing effective accounting and
information systems; controlling price-sensitive information affecting the Company’s share price; and ensuring prompt and
timely communication with the Company’s stakeholders.

The Company is committed to establishing and maintaining risk management and internal control systems, including
policies and procedures that the Company considers appropriate for its business operations. The Company is dedicated
to continuously improving such systems and fostering a risk management culture to enhance risk management awareness
among all employees.

The Company has adopted and implemented comprehensive risk management policies across all aspects of its business
operations. In particular, the Company:

•      established an Investment Decision-Making Committee and a Risk Management Department to oversee external
       investment and compliance management risks;

•      provided ongoing anti-corruption and anti-bribery compliance training to employees to enhance their awareness of
       legal and regulatory requirements and encourage compliance with such requirements in their daily work; and

•      arranged for the Directors and senior management to attend training seminars on the Listing Rules and the
       responsibilities of directors of a listed company in Hong Kong.

The Company has established an internal audit function, the effectiveness of which was reviewed by the Audit Committee
during the year.

reviewed the effectiveness of the Company’s risk management and internal control systems, including the adequacy of
resources, staff qualifications and experience, training programs and whether the budget is sufficient of the Company’s
accounting and financial reporting function, covering financial controls, operational controls, compliance monitoring and

     Whistleblowing Policy

     The Company has put in place a whistleblowing policy and a whistleblowing mailbox, under which employees and other
     stakeholders of the Company can, in a confidential and anonymous manner, raise concerns to the Audit Committee
     regarding any possible impropriety in relation to the Company.

     Anti-Bribery and Anti-Corruption Policy

     The Company has formulated an anti-corruption risk management policy, continuously updating and issuing the Detailed
     Implementation Rules for Integrity Construction Management, which prohibit employees from engaging in any corrupt
     activities, whether for personal gain or improper advantage. Our internal audit department is directly responsible for anti-
     corruption risk management, establishing an integrity employment management system, and has set up an anti-corruption
     committee comprising designated personnel from the Human Resources, Internal Audit, and Legal Departments. We have
     established a whistleblowing mechanism that encourages internal reporting of suspicious activities. We adopt a zero-
     tolerance policy towards corrupt conduct and do not accept the hiring or promotion of individuals involved in corrupt
     incidents. We conduct regular internal training and require all suppliers and partners to comply with compliance standards
     and sign professional integrity agreements.

     During the Reporting Period, neither the employees nor material business partners of the Company were subject to,
     or otherwise involved in, any complaints, investigations or regulatory enquiries concerning any bribery or kickback
     arrangements.

     Disclosure of Inside Information

     In order to enhance the Company’s system for handling and disseminating inside information and to ensure the
     truthfulness, accuracy, completeness and timeliness of its public disclosures, the Company has adopted and implemented
     policies and procedures for inside information. The Company has taken reasonable measures from time to time to
     ensure that potential inside information is captured and kept confidential, so as to prevent any breach of the disclosure
     requirements in relation to the Company. These measures include, among others:

     •      restricting access to information to a limited number of employees on a need-to-know basis. Employees who are in
            possession of inside information are fully aware of their confidentiality obligations.

     •      entering into confidentiality agreements when the Company engages in material negotiations.

     •      designating executive directors or relevant persons as authorized spokespersons to communicate with external
            parties such as the media, analysts or investors, and to respond to external enquiries on behalf of the Company.

8. REPORT OF THE BOARD



Principal Risks and Uncertainties
The following are the principal risks and uncertainties faced by the Group, some of which are beyond its control:

1.     Market and Industry Risks

       •      Our future growth depends on customer demand for and willingness to adopt new energy-powered
              construction machinery. The management will continuously enrich the new energy product portfolio,
              strengthen promotion and customized services in key markets, boost customers’ willingness to replace
              equipment with new energy equipment, and consolidate the foundation of end-market demand.

       •      The new energy construction machinery industry is highly competitive, and we cannot guarantee success
              in competing within these industries. The management will consolidate differentiated advantages in core
              technologies including the three-electric system and unmanned operation, focus on in-depth operation in
              segmented application scenarios, and steadily enhance the industry competitiveness.

       •      The industry in which we operate is characterized by rapid technological iteration and development.
              The adoption of any alternative energy sources, including hydrogen energy, may adversely affect our
              business, prospects, operating results and financial condition. The management will conduct research and
              development across multiple technological routes and keep pace with industry trends of pure electric and
              hybrid technologies, mitigating operational risks arising from over-reliance on a single technical route.

2.     Operational and Financial Risks

       •      During the Reporting Period, we recorded net losses and net cash used in operating activities, and we may
              incur net losses and net cash used in operating activities in the future. The management will strictly control
              operating expenses and optimize order structure and receivables collection management, steadily improving
              overall cash flow and profitability.

       •      We recorded relatively low gross profit margins during the Reporting Period. The management will reduce
              costs and improve efficiency by optimizing the supply chain system, scale up the development of high value-
              added products, and gradually restoring profitability.

       •      Our product prices may fluctuate, which could have an adverse effect on our business, prospects, operating
              results and financial condition. The management will mitigate the impact of industry price volatility by
              securing long-term cooperation orders and upgrading product value.

       •      Our products may not meet customers’ performance expectations and may have potential defects. The
              management will continuously optimize product stability and user experience by improving the R&D testing
              and full-process quality control system and upgrading the after-sales service system.

       •      Our business plans require substantial funding. To meet our future funding needs, we may need to seek
              equity or debt financing, which could dilute our shareholders’ equity and introduce covenants that restrict
              our operations or dividend payments. However, such funding may not be available on acceptable terms, in
              a timely manner, or at all, which could force us to scale back or cease our operations. The management
              will control financial constraint risks by rationally planning capital utilization, expanding diversified financing
              channels and balancing the financing structure.
60   BRETON TECHNOLOGY CO., LTD.       2025 ANNUAL REPORT




     8. REPORT OF THE BOARD



     3.     Corporate Development and Product Mix Risks

            •      Our limited operating history makes it difficult to assess our future prospects. The management will build
                   brand reputation and industry recognition over time through prudent operation, standardized internal
                   governance and information disclosure.

            •      During the Reporting Period and for the foreseeable future, our revenue has primarily depended on and will
                   continue to depend on a limited number of models. The management will accelerate the R&D and mass
                   production of multi-scenario and multi-category new energy equipment to mitigate operational risks caused
                   by concentrated reliance on a single product.

     The above does not constitute an exhaustive list of the risks faced by the Group. For other risks and uncertainties faced by
     the Group, please refer to the section headed “Risk Factors” in the Prospectus.

     The Company considers that risk management is crucial to the efficient and effective operation of the Group. The
     management of the Company assists the Board in evaluating material internal and external risks arising from the Group’s
     business, including operational risks, financial risks, regulatory risks, among others, and takes proactive steps to establish
     appropriate risk management and internal control mechanisms and integrate them into daily operational management.

8. REPORT OF THE BOARD



Environmental Policy and Performance

The Group has consistently prioritized monitoring its Environmental, Social and Governance (“ESG”) practices and is
committed to integrating them into its daily operations. The Group recognizes its obligations towards environmental
protection and corporate social responsibility, and firmly believes that the integration of ESG principles is vital to the
Group’s long-term business growth.

The Group has formulated an ESG policy, which includes the following elements: (i) effective risk governance relating
to ESG matters, including environmental, social and climate-related risks; (ii) identification of key stakeholders and
establishment of communication channels with them; (iii) a clear ESG framework; (iv) procedures for formulating ESG
strategies; (v) ESG risk management and monitoring protocols; and (vi) identification of key performance indicators (“KPIs”),
together with relevant initiatives and mitigation measures. The Board has the overall responsibility for reviewing, approving
and adopting the ESG policy as well as the Group’s targets and strategies. Furthermore, it will oversee the implementation
of the ESG policy, make necessary revisions to the overall ESG strategies as it deems appropriate, and effectively manage
other material ESG-related matters.

Further details regarding the Company’s environmental policy and performance, please refer to the section “9. 2025
Environmental, Social and Governance Report” set out on page 85 to page 148 of this annual report.

Compliance with Relevant Laws and Regulations

The operations of the Group are required to comply with applicable PRC laws and regulations. For the year ended
December 31, 2025, to the best of the Board’s knowledge, the Group has not been involved in any non-compliance
incidents that resulted in fines, enforcement actions or other penalties, which, individually or in the aggregate, could have
a material adverse effect on the Group’s business, financial condition or results of operations, and has complied, in all
material respects, with relevant laws and regulations that have a significant impact on the Group.

Relationship with Employees, Customers and Suppliers

The Group recognises the importance of maintaining good relationships with its employees, customers and suppliers in
achieving its current and long-term goals. The Group maintains good relationships with its employees, customers and
suppliers. For the year ended December 31, 2025, there were no significant or serious disputes between the Group and its
employees, customers and/or suppliers.

For further details regarding the Company’s relationships with its employees and suppliers, please refer to the section “9.
2025 Environmental, Social and Governance Report” on pages 85 to 148 of this report.
62   BRETON TECHNOLOGY CO., LTD.       2025 ANNUAL REPORT




     8. REPORT OF THE BOARD



     Employees

     As of December 31, 2025, we had a total of 346 employees. We utilize a variety of recruiting channels to sustain
     our robust talent pool and support our continued growth. Our primary recruitment methods include online platforms,
     employment agencies and referrals. We provide a series of training for new employees, covering technological knowledge,
     skills development, corporate culture, leadership and communication to prepare them as future leaders of our Company.
     The Group is committed to establishing a competitive and fair remuneration package. To effectively motivate employees,
     the Group continuously improves its remuneration and incentive policies through market research. The Group conducts
     annual performance appraisals for its employees to provide feedback on their performance. Employee compensation
     generally consists of base salary and performance-based bonuses. In compliance with applicable PRC laws and
     regulations, we participate in a variety of government statutory employee benefit plans, including social insurance, namely
     pension insurance, medical insurance, unemployment insurance, work-related injury insurance, and maternity insurance, as
     well as housing funds. The Group make contributions to employee benefit plans at specified percentages of the salaries,
     bonuses and certain allowances of our employees as required by applicable PRC laws and regulations, up to an upper limit
     stipulated by the local government regulations. Additionally, we provide employer’s liability insurance and supplementary
     commercial accident insurance to enhance our employees’ coverage.

     Customers

     The Group continuously strives to enhance customer satisfaction by providing quality customer service. The Group has
     a customer service department that conducts follow-up interviews to assess the quality of services provided, as well
     as customers’ suggestions and comments. Customers may submit complaints orally or in writing via designated email
     addresses or hotlines.

     Suppliers

     When selecting suppliers, the Group considers factors such as customer demand for specific equipment and related
     specifications, functions or models to identify a pool of suppliers capable of meeting such demand. Furthermore, leveraging
     the Group’s extensive experience in the mining industry and in-depth understanding of various mining equipment, the
     Group has established comprehensive supply chain management capabilities, enabling it to select the most suitable
     suppliers with strong service capabilities, a proven track record and favourable cooperation terms, thereby ensuring the
     quality of products and services provided by the Group and stability of supply.

     Major Customers and Suppliers

     For the year ended December 31, 2025, revenue attributable to the Group’s five largest customers accounted for
     approximately 35.44% of the Group’s revenue from the sale of goods or provision of services. For the year ended
     December 31, 2025, revenue attributable to the Group’s largest customer accounted for approximately 7.96% of the
     Group’s revenue from the sale of goods or provision of services.

     Employee Incentive Schemes

     In recognition of the contributions of the relevant persons (primarily comprise our employees) and to incentivize them to
     further promote our development, Shanghai Jifang, Shanghai Fangzhanbo and Shanghai Fangao, were established in the
     PRC as our incentive platforms (the “Incentive Platforms”). For further details of the Incentive Platforms, see “History,
     Development and Corporate Structure — Incentive Platforms” in the Prospectus. We have adopted our employee incentive
     schemes in respect of the Incentive Platforms (the “Employee Incentive Schemes”). For details of the Employee Incentive
     Schemes, please refer to “Statutory and General Information – C. Further Information about Our Directors, Supervisors and
     Substantial Shareholders – 5. Employee Incentive Schemes” in Appendix VI to the Prospectus. The Employee Incentive
     Schemes are not subject to the provisions of Chapter 17 of the Listing Rules as they do not involve the grant of options
     or share awards by our Company to subscribe for the Shares after the Listing. As of the Latest Practicable Date, given
     the underlying Shares under the Employee Incentive Schemes had already been issued to the Incentive Platforms, there
     will not be any dilution effect to the issued Shares. The selected participants under the Employee Incentive Schemes (the
     “Participants”) are granted awards in the form of economic interests in the Incentive Platforms and become indirectly
     interested in our Company through their respective interests as limited partners of the relevant Incentive Platforms upon
     acquisition of partnership interests in the relevant Incentive Platforms.

8. REPORT OF THE BOARD



Pension Scheme/Retirement Benefits Scheme
Pursuant to the relevant PRC rules and regulations, employees of the Group based in the PRC participate in various
defined contribution retirement benefit schemes organized by the relevant municipal and provincial governments under
which the Group and the employees are required to make monthly contributions to these schemes at a certain percentage
of the employees’ salaries (subject to certain caps). The municipal and provincial governments have undertaken to assume
the responsibility for the retirement benefits obligations payable to all existing and future retired employees under the
schemes mentioned above in the PRC. Other than the monthly contributions, the Group has no obligation for the payment
of retirement and other post-retirement benefits of its employees. The assets of these schemes are held separately
from the assets of the Group in independently administered funds administered by the PRC government. The Group’s
contributions to these schemes are expensed as incurred.

For the year ended 31 December 2025, the Group has no right to utilize the forfeited contributions under the aforesaid
social insurance and housing fund schemes to reduce the existing contribution levels, and accordingly, no such
contributions have been utilized. During the year ended 31 December 2025, the Group maintained no defined benefit
plans.

Details of these retirement schemes are set out in Note 6(b) to the consolidated financial statements.

Remuneration Policy
The remuneration of the Directors and the Group’s senior management is determined by the Board with reference to their
respective responsibilities, duties, experience, individual performance and time commitment to the Group, and may be
adjusted based on recommendations from the Remuneration and Appraisal Committee. The Remuneration and Appraisal
Committee was established to consider the Company’s remuneration policy and all remuneration structures for the
Company’s Directors and senior management.

Remuneration of Directors, Supervisors and the Five Highest-Paid Individuals
The Directors, Supervisors and senior management members who receive remuneration from the Company are paid in
the forms of salaries and other benefits in kind, discretionary bonuses, retirement benefit scheme contributions and share-
based payment. The remuneration of the Directors, Supervisors and senior management members is determined with
reference to the remuneration paid by comparable companies and the achievement of major operating indicators of the
Company.

Details of the remuneration of the Directors and the five highest paid individuals of the Group are set out in Notes 8 and 9
to the consolidated financial statements in this Annual Report.

For the year ended December 31, 2025, no remuneration was paid or payable by the Company to any Director, Supervisor
or the five highest paid individuals as an inducement to join or upon joining the Company. For the year ended December
31, 2025, no compensation was paid or payable by the Company to any Director, former Director, Supervisor, former
Supervisor or the five highest paid individuals for the loss of office in connection with the management of the affairs of any
subsidiary of the Company.

For the year ended December 31, 2025, no Director or Supervisor has waived or agreed to waive any remuneration
or benefits in kind. Save as disclosed above, for the year ended December 31, 2025, no other payments were paid or
payable by the Company or any of its subsidiaries to the Directors, Supervisors or the five highest paid individuals.

     Connected Transactions

     From the Listing Date up to December 31, 2025, the Company has strictly complied with the requirements of Chapter 14A
     of the Listing Rules regarding its connected transactions. Details of the non-exempt connected transactions entered into
     by the Company from the Listing Date up to December 31, 2025 are set out below.

     Entering into the Breton Smart Electric Capital Increase Agreement

     On June 4, 2025, the Company, Breton Smart Electric (博雷頓智電), formerly known as Baipin (Shanghai) Intelligent
     Technology Co., Ltd., a subsidiary of the Company, and Shanghai Yijin entered into a capital increase agreement (the
     “Breton Smart Electric Capital Increase Agreement”). Shanghai Yijin is one of the Company’s controlling shareholders
     and is a connected person of the Company under Chapter 14A of the Listing Rules. Accordingly, the transactions
     contemplated under the Capital Increase Agreement constitute connected transactions of the Company under Chapter
     14A of the Listing Rules. Pursuant to the Breton Smart Electric Capital Increase Agreement, Shanghai Yijin will contribute
     RMB20 million to increase the capital and expand the shareholding of Breton Smart Electric, and the registered capital
     of Breton Smart Electric is proposed to be increased from RMB40 million to RMB60 million. Prior to this capital increase,
     Breton Smart Electric was a wholly-owned subsidiary of the Company. Upon completion of this capital increase, the
     Company’s shareholding in Breton Smart Electric will decrease to approximately 66.67%, and Breton Smart Electric
     will remain a subsidiary of the Company and will continue to be consolidated into the Group’s consolidated financial
     statements.

     This capital increase:

     (1)    was agreed through negotiation among the parties, with the price for this capital increase being RMB1 per RMB1 of
            registered capital.

     (2)    The parties confirmed that Shanghai Yijin will contribute capital in cash, with this capital increase amount being
            RMB20 million, to subscribe for the newly issued registered capital of Breton Smart Electric of RMB20 million, of
            which RMB20 million will be recorded in the registered capital of Breton Smart Electric, and RMB0 will be recorded
            in the capital reserve of Breton Smart Electric.

     (3)    During this capital increase, the Company waived its pre-emptive rights for this capital increase, regardless of
            whether such rights were obtained based on legal provisions, the articles of association or any other reasons.

     (4)    Breton Smart Electric shall submit the industrial and commercial change materials for this capital increase to the
            industrial and commercial registration authority within ten working days after Shanghai Yijin pays the full capital
            contribution amount, and shall provide Shanghai Yijin with a copy of the amended business license of the enterprise
            legal person affixed with Breton Smart Electric’s official seal, the original capital contribution certificate and the
            register of members as soon as possible. Upon completion of the industrial and commercial change registration,
            the capital increase will be deemed to have been completed (the “Completion Date”). Shanghai Yijin shall become
            a shareholder of Breton Smart Electric from the Completion Date, enjoying and bearing the shareholder rights and
            obligations stipulated in Breton Smart Electric’s articles of association and PRC laws.
                                                                     2025 ANNUAL REPORT      BRETON TECHNOLOGY CO., LTD.        79




8. REPORT OF THE BOARD


(5)    After the Breton Smart Electric Capital Increase Agreement is signed and becomes effective, the parties shall
       cooperate with each other to handle relevant procedures. Shanghai Yijin agreed to fully contribute the capital
       increase amount, totaling RMB20 million, to Breton Smart Electric’s designated account by August 3, 2025.

The Breton Smart Electric Capital Increase Agreement became effective upon signing by the parties and approval by the
shareholders’ meeting of Breton Smart Electric.

Pursuant to the Breton Smart Electric Capital Increase Agreement, the price for Shanghai Yijin’s subscription for the newly
increased registered capital of Breton Smart Electric is RMB1 per RMB1 of registered capital. This pricing was determined
through joint negotiation among the parties to this capital increase based on the principles of voluntariness, fairness and
justice, taking into account Breton Smart Electric’s financial position as at December 31, 2024 as well as its operational
needs, future development and funding arrangements.

This capital increase is in line with the strategic development plans of the Company and Breton Smart Electric, and will help
Breton Smart Electric enhance its capital strength and optimise its shareholding structure. This move will further strengthen
the synergy and support between the Company and Breton Smart Electric, support its achievement of overseas business
and globalisation goals, enhance its independent development capabilities, and ultimately enhance the Company’s core
competitiveness and promote long-term high-quality development.

The Directors (including the independent non-executive Directors) confirmed that (i) the terms of the Breton Smart Electric
Capital Increase Agreement are fair and reasonable; (ii) the transactions contemplated under the Breton Smart Electric
Capital Increase Agreement were entered into in the ordinary and usual course of business of the Group, and the terms
of the Capital Increase Agreement were on normal commercial terms or better, and in the ordinary and usual course of
business; and (iii) this capital increase is in the interests of the Company and its shareholders as a whole.

For details of the Breton Smart Electric Capital Increase Agreement and the transactions thereunder, please refer to the
Company’s announcement dated June 4, 2025.

Entering into the Breton Intelligent Capital Increase Agreement

On December 2, 2025, the Company, Breton Intelligent, a wholly-owned subsidiary of the Company, Breton Investment,
Mr. Bai Furu, Mr. Cui Hongyang and Mr. Huang Haike entered into a capital increase agreement (the “Breton Intelligent
Capital Increase Agreement”). Breton Investment is one of the Company’s controlling shareholders and is a connected
person of the Company under Chapter 14A of the Listing Rules. Accordingly, the transactions contemplated under the
Breton Intelligent Capital Increase Agreement constitute connected transactions of the Company under Chapter 14A of the
Listing Rules. Pursuant to the Breton Intelligent Capital Increase Agreement, the Company will contribute RMB24 million in
cash, Breton Investment will contribute RMB3 million in cash, Mr. Cui Hongyang will contribute RMB6 million in cash, Mr.
Huang Haike will contribute RMB4 million in cash and Mr. Bai Furu will contribute RMB3 million in cash, for a total cash
contribution of RMB40 million to increase the capital and expand the shareholding of Breton Intelligent.
80   BRETON TECHNOLOGY CO., LTD.        2025 ANNUAL REPORT




     8. REPORT OF THE BOARD


     Prior to this capital increase, Breton Intelligent was a wholly-owned subsidiary of the Company. Upon completion of this
     capital increase, the registered capital of Breton Intelligent will increase from RMB10 million to RMB50 million, and Breton
     Intelligent will be held approximately 68% by the Company, approximately 6% by Breton Investment, approximately
     12% by Mr. Cui Hongyang, approximately 8% by Mr. Huang Haike and approximately 6% by Mr. Bai Furu. Although the
     Company’s shareholding in Breton Intelligent will decrease from 100% to approximately 68%, Breton Intelligent will remain
     a subsidiary of the Company and will be consolidated into the Group’s consolidated financial statements.

     This capital increase:

     (1)    was agreed through negotiation among the parties, that the registered capital of Breton Intelligent will be increased
            from RMB10 million to RMB50 million, with the new increase being RMB40 million;

     (2)    The price for the capital increase is RMB1 per RMB1 of registered capital;

     (3)    Each party will contribute capital in cash, with the Company contributing RMB24 million from its own funds, Breton
            Investment contributing RMB3 million, Mr. Cui Hongyang contributing RMB6 million, Mr. Huang Haike contributing
            RMB4 million and Mr. Bai Furu contributing RMB3 million, for a total contribution of RMB40 million, among which,
            RMB40 million will be recorded in Breton Intelligent’s registered capital, and RMB0 will be recorded in Breton
            Intelligent’s capital reserve;

     (4)    The parties agree to pay the capital contribution amount in a lump sum to Breton Intelligent’s account within 5
            working days after Breton Intelligent Capital Increase Agreement becomes effective;

     (5)    Breton Intelligent shall submit the industrial and commercial change materials for this capital increase to the
            industrial and commercial registration authority within 30 days after each investor pays the full capital contribution
            amount, and shall provide the new shareholders with a copy of the business license affixed with its official seal,
            capital contribution certificates and other materials. The date of completion of the industrial and commercial change
            shall be the Completion Date;

     The Breton Intelligent Capital Increase Agreement shall become effective upon signing by the parties and approval by the
     general meeting of Breton Intelligent.

     The capital contribution paid by the Company in this capital increase was funded by the net proceeds from the Company’s
     global offering.

     Pursuant to the Breton Intelligent Capital Increase Agreement, all investors will contribute capital at a price of RMB1 per
     RMB1 of registered capital. This price was determined through negotiation among the parties to this capital increase based
     on the principles of voluntariness, fairness and justice, taking into account the financial position of each party and Breton
     Intelligent as at the signing date of the Breton Intelligent Capital Increase Agreement, and Breton Intelligent’s funding needs
     for technology research and development and deployment implementation in the field of unmanned dump trucks in mining
     sites.
                                                                       2025 ANNUAL REPORT       BRETON TECHNOLOGY CO., LTD.         81




8. REPORT OF THE BOARD


This capital increase will help enhance Breton Intelligent’s capital strength, lay a solid financial foundation for its promotion
of intelligent mining and automation-related businesses, and is also in line with the strategic development plans of Breton
Investment and other investors. At the same time, the introduction of external funds can further optimise its shareholding
structure, enhance the Company’s governance level, promote its long-term stable development, and ultimately enhance
the Company’s core competitiveness and promote its long-term high-quality development. Although the Company’s
shareholding in Breton Intelligent will decrease, Breton Intelligent will remain a subsidiary of the Company and will be
consolidated into the Group’s consolidated financial statements. This capital increase will not have a material adverse
effect on the Company’s financial position and business.

As disclosed in the “Future Plans and Use of Proceeds” section of the Prospectus, approximately 20% of the net
proceeds, or HK$29.5 million, will be used to develop a complete set of scenario-specific products and solutions equipped
with intelligent technologies, as well as advanced systems such as an AI expert service support system, an automatic
operating system utilising cloud computing and edge computing, and roadside systems for automatic cluster operations.
Under this plan, this capital increase clarifies that the Group will take Breton Intelligent as the core entity focusing on
technology research and development and deployment of unmanned dump trucks in mining sites, leveraging and
integrating resources to invest in the research and development of a new generation of unmanned vehicles and dispatching
systems, and developing a complete set of scenario-specific products and solutions equipped with intelligent technologies
and an AI expert service support system. At the same time, Breton Intelligent will continue to strengthen its R&D talent
system construction, recruiting R&D experts in fields such as algorithms and machine learning to comprehensively enhance
its core technical capabilities.

The Directors (including the independent non-executive Directors) confirm that (i) the terms of the Breton Intelligent
Capital Increase Agreement are fair and reasonable; (ii) the terms of the Breton Intelligent Capital Increase Agreement are
entered into on normal commercial terms or better; and (iii) the Capital Increase is in the interests of the Company and its
shareholders as a whole.

For details of the Breton Intelligent Capital Increase Agreement and the transactions thereunder, please refer to the
Company’s announcement dated December 2, 2025.

subsidiaries, nor was there any material contract for the provision of services to the Company or any of its subsidiaries by
the controlling shareholders or any of their subsidiaries.

     Board Briefing

     Chairman’s Statement
     Based on ESG Foundations, Build Long-Term Value Through New Quality Productivity and Global
     Development

     Over the past year, the global industrial landscape has accelerated its restructuring. New quality productivity, centred on
     technological innovation, continues to drive the upgrading of traditional industries, with green, low-carbon and intelligent
     approaches becoming key directions for industrial development. Corporate competitiveness increasingly manifests in the
     integrated levels of innovation capability, resource efficiency, and sustainable development. Environmental, Social and
     Governance (ESG) principles are becoming deeply embedded in corporate management, emerging as a pivotal force in
     enhancing the quality of growth.

     Breton Technology Co., Ltd. (“the Company”) continues to integrate ESG principles into its development strategy,
     focusing on mining electrification, energy cleanliness, and intelligent technologies to lead the industry towards upgrades
     characterised by higher efficiency, lower energy consumption, and enhanced safety. Through technological innovation and
     scenario-based applications, the Company continuously enhances product competitiveness and solution value, creating
     sustainable economic benefits for customers while driving the industry’s transition towards green development.

     New-type productive forces inject core momentum into the Company’s development. Leveraging new energy technologies,
     intelligent equipment, automated systems, and digital capabilities, the Company is propelling the mining sector towards a
     phase of high-tech, high-efficiency, and high-quality development. The continuous enhancement of innovation capacity not
     only yields novel product forms and service models but also continually expands the boundaries of corporate growth.

     Within the globalisation process, the Company persistently advances its “going global” strategy, extending innovative
     technologies and systematic solutions to broader international markets. The global mining sector’s growing demand for
     clean energy and intelligent upgrades has opened new development opportunities for the enterprise. By participating in
     global industrial collaboration, the Company not only drives business growth but also introduces green technologies and
     efficient operational models to more regions, injecting fresh momentum into the global mining industry’s upgrading and
     sustainable development.

     The value generated by ESG is reflected in revenue growth, cost optimisation, and efficiency improvements, as well as in
     enhanced corporate governance capabilities and long-term developmental resilience. By continuously refining management
     systems, strengthening risk control, and enhancing cross-regional coordination, the Company continually elevates its global
     operational capabilities, laying the foundation for sustained, robust development.

     Looking ahead, the Company will persistently advance its ESG development philosophy, with new-quality productive forces
     as the core driver. It will resolutely pursue global expansion, continuously propel innovation and integration within the
     energy and resources sectors, and contribute enduring strength to global green development while creating commercial
     value.
                                                                     2025 ANNUAL REPORT      BRETON TECHNOLOGY CO., LTD.        87




9. 2025 ENVIRONMENTAL, SOCIAL AND GOVERNANCE REPORT



ESG Strategy Overview and Corporate Culture

Since establishing the development of “zero-carbon mining robots” as a key strategic objective and elevating its status,
the Company has clearly defined its future direction and consolidated the foundations for sustainable development. We
actively maintain a balance between commercial value, social value, and shareholder value, achieving sustainable corporate
development through steady progress while fully considering the interests of shareholders and all stakeholders.

In realising commercial value, the Company prioritises user value, investing substantially in advancing cutting-edge
technology and product R&D capabilities. We reinforce safeguards for user privacy and data security, delivering premium
and innovative product experiences. We facilitate industrial digital transformation, co-create social value, and share
commercial outcomes arising from the convergence of digital and physical economies.

In pioneering social value, the Company focuses on developing innovative technological and product solutions to alleviate
societal challenges. We guide business units in identifying and enhancing the social value of products and operations,
collaborating with specialised non-profit organisations across key sectors to incubate projects delivering scalable societal
benefits.

Regarding shareholder value, the Company remains steadfast in creating user value while organically integrating social
and commercial value. This approach solidifies the foundation for sustainable development and enhances the enterprise’s
long-term worth. During the reporting period, the Company was honoured with the titles of “Minhang District Enterprise
Technology Centre” and “Shanghai Manufacturing Single Champion” for its outstanding technological innovation capabilities
and industrial contributions. Concurrently, it successfully passed the re-evaluation for its “Specialized, Sophisticated,
Differential, and Innovative SMEs” Little Giants Enterprise status and Shanghai High-Tech Enterprise certification. Moreover,
its subsidiary, Breton (Hunan) Technology Co., Ltd., was successfully recognised as both a High-Tech Enterprise and an
Innovative Enterprise. These achievements fully demonstrate the company’s sustained accumulation in core technological
domains and its market-leading position, further fortifying its competitive resilience and growth potential.

Breton has established the development of “zero-carbon mining robots” as a key strategic objective, steadfastly
implementing a development philosophy centred on “green, intelligent, efficiency-enhancing practices and the harmonious
coexistence of humanity and nature”. The company consistently leads the mining industry’s green transformation through
technological innovation, focusing on three core domains: clean energy, electric equipment, and unmanned operations.
This drives high-energy consumption and high-emission scenarios towards zero-carbon operations. The company
implements systematic deployment across environmental, social, and governance dimensions, encompassing carbon
reduction target setting, green manufacturing and energy conservation management, employee rights protection, supply
chain compliance management, and governance structure optimisation. This holistic approach strives to unify economic,
environmental, and social value. The company positions ESG as the strategic engine driving its long-term, high-quality
development. It continuously strengthens transparent governance, innovation capabilities, and sustainable resilience,
striving to become the global leader in zero-carbon mining robotics. This commitment contributes to both China’s dual
carbon goals and global sustainable development.

     continuous refinement of ESG management mechanisms. While ensuring the achievement of the Company’s operational
     objectives, the Company actively responds to the expectations of shareholders and stakeholders, earnestly fulfils its
     corporate social responsibilities, and continuously creates long-term value for society, thereby safeguarding the Company’s
     sustainable and high-quality development.

ESG Materiality Assessment

The company identified 21 key ESG issues by referencing the Global Sustainable Development Goals, exchange
requirements, investor concerns, domestic and international policies, and integrating stakeholder feedback with the
company’s overall strategy. Through systematic research and analysis, we assessed the level of concern among internal
and external stakeholders regarding each ESG issue and prioritised the importance of key ESG issues. This year, we
evaluated 21 material issues.

Our materiality assessment process comprises the following steps:


Identify issues and establish a    Collect ESG-related issues based on our industry context and core business activities,
materiality issue database         incorporating input from the Board and relevant departments to preliminarily identify
                                   and screen ESG issues closely linked to our operations.

Materiality assessment and         Engage with stakeholders and experts to evaluate the material impact of ESG issues.
prioritisation                     Through internal and external stakeholder surveys, we assess the importance of ESG
                                   issues across two dimensions: “significance to the Company’s development” and
                                   “significance to stakeholders”. This determines priority levels, with a materiality matrix
                                   plotted based on actual research findings.

Certification of Materiality       We synthesise internal and external assessments of issue materiality, integrating these
Assessment Results                 with our strategic considerations to obtain final approval for material ESG issues from
                                   the Board of Directors and the ESG Working Group.

1.     About This Report

1.1 About This Report

Breton and its subsidiaries (“Breton”, “the Group” or “we”) are pleased to present the inaugural ESG Report (“this Report”).
This Report aims to transparently disclose the Group’s progress and achievements within the ESG domain across its
actual business operations. It demonstrates the Group’s sustainability vision to stakeholders, thereby deepening their
understanding of the company and strengthening their confidence.

Basis and Scope of Preparation

This Report has been prepared in accordance with Appendix C2, the ESG Reporting Code (“ESG Code”) of the Listing
Rules of The Stock Exchange of Hong Kong Limited (“SEHK”) and the United Nations Sustainable Development Goals (UN
SDGs). It follows the “comply or explain” principle in addressing relevant matters and presents environmental and social
key performance indicators as required by the ESG Code for performance review.

with the financial year covered in the Group’s 2025 Annual Report. All information covered in this report is consistent with
the reporting scope of the Group’s 2025 Annual Report.

2.    Enhancing Responsible Governance to Safeguard Sustainable Development

United Nations Sustainable Development Goals (UNSDGs) Corresponding to This Chapter




The Company has established a robust top-level oversight framework for governance, deeply embedding sustainable
development principles within its governance system. This effectively guides the practical implementation across all
business units, providing a solid foundation for the enterprise’s sustainable development.
96   BRETON TECHNOLOGY CO., LTD.      2025 ANNUAL REPORT




     9. 2025 ENVIRONMENTAL, SOCIAL AND GOVERNANCE REPORT



     2.1 Sustainable Governance

     Sound and effective governance is the cornerstone of a company’s stable development. The Company implements
     corporate governance in compliance with laws, regulations, and institutional requirements including the Company Law of
     the People’s Republic of China and the Rules Governing the Listing of Securities on The Stock Exchange of Hong Kong
     Limited. Detailed information regarding the Company’s corporate governance is available in the “Corporate Governance
     Report” section of the Company’s Annual Report. In April 2025, the Board of Directors reviewed and approved the Breton
     Technology Co., Ltd. ESG Management System, establishing a structured ESG management framework.

     The Board plays a pivotal role in guiding the Company’s strategic direction, overseeing corporate governance, and
     ensuring long-term sustainable development. As of 31 December 2025, the Board comprised ten directors, including four
     executive directors, two non-executive directors, and four independent non-executive directors. The gender ratio (male:
     female) on the Board was nine to one.

     Nine board meetings were convened in 2025, with an overall attendance rate of 98% (95% in 2024).

     The Company has established a three-tier ESG management structure comprising an ESG Committee (an internal
     organisational body authorised by the Board), an ESG Office, and an ESG Working Group. The Board has also implemented
     measures to enhance the transparency of business reporting, enabling stakeholders to gain a deeper understanding of the
     Group’s strategies and objectives.

     The ESG Committee, serving as the oversight and management body for the company’s ESG initiatives, is responsible for
     supervising and managing the company’s ESG management strategies, policies, and performance, reporting regularly to
     the Board of Directors. The ESG Committee is accountable to the Board, with its proposals subject to Board deliberation
     and decision. The Committee formulates the company’s medium-to-long-term and annual ESG strategies, visionary
     objectives, planning, policy frameworks, assessment and improvement mechanisms, organisational structures, and
     disclosure requirements. Upon approval by the Board of Directors, these become guiding documents for the Company’s
     ESG management. The Group places high importance on the suggestions and opinions of all stakeholders, establishing
     robust communication channels to engage in ongoing dialogue with key stakeholders. Based on these discussions,
     the Group identifies material ESG issues and potential ESG risks, continuously refining its ESG strategies and policy
     frameworks. During the reporting year, the Board reviewed material ESG issues and approved proposals to adjust the
     materiality levels of various ESG topics, ensuring the timeliness and reasonableness of the materiality matrix.

     The Board convenes regular meetings to deliberate and approve the company’s sustainability objectives, assess and
     review ESG-related targets, and ensure alignment with the company’s overall business strategy and development direction.
     We guide and oversee the formulation and implementation of the company’s ESG vision, strategy and framework, review
     the company’s material ESG issues, key ESG risks and opportunities, monitor communication channels and methods with
     shareholders, and review the company’s ESG-related disclosures.
                                                                     2025 ANNUAL REPORT       BRETON TECHNOLOGY CO., LTD.        97




9. 2025 ENVIRONMENTAL, SOCIAL AND GOVERNANCE REPORT


The ESG Office is responsible for implementing and executing the ESG Committee’s action plans in the area of
environmental, social and governance (ESG) matters. It coordinates the ESG Working Group, formed by relevant
departments, to jointly execute specific ESG-related measures. Under the guidance of the ESG Committee, the ESG
Office develops detailed work plans in accordance with ESG requirements, oversees the ESG Working Group in deploying
specific tasks, and regularly organises assessments of ESG performance and management, culminating in the preparation
of reports. The ESG Working Group is responsible for implementing specific ESG measures. Comprising heads of
functional departments including Production, Human Resources, Central Research Department, Public Relations, Sales,
and the Board Secretariat Office, the Working Group facilitates collaboration among company departments, subsidiaries,
and third-party consultancy firms. It provides specific guidance for ESG measures within annual targets and compiles the
annual ESG report. We require each subsidiary to designate a dedicated individual responsible for ESG data management,
ensuring timely updates, collation, and archiving of data, alongside providing data verification or source documentation. At
year-end, our subsidiaries compile all annually submitted data, which is then reviewed, signed off by responsible personnel
from each subsidiary, and submitted to the ESG Working Group.

Additionally, we engage a reputable consultancy firm as our third-party ESG advisor to assist in monitoring and adhering to
ESG policy requirements, and to assess our performance in environmental protection and climate change mitigation.

2.2 Business Ethics and Anti-Corruption

The Company strictly adheres to laws and regulations, continuously refines corporate governance mechanisms and internal
management systems, conducts ideological and ethical awareness training, implements oversight and inspections for
integrity and compliance, and consistently holds itself and its employees to the highest standards of business ethics. We
are committed to fostering a team environment characterised by integrity, probity, and proactive initiative.

The Company is currently developing its internal management systems and has drafted the “Breton Technology Co., Ltd.
Anti-Sanctions and Financial Crime Prevention Management System (Revised Draft)”, aimed at regulating anti-sanctions
and financial crime activities.

The Company has established the “Implementation Rules for Integrity Management”, applicable to all employees and
subsidiaries. An Integrity Management Task Force has been formed, chaired by the Chairman, with members appointed
by the Chairperson. This Task Force is responsible for formulating, revising, issuing, and interpreting integrity policies and
related systems, as well as organising integrity and self-discipline training. The Integrity Management Task Force Team
shall conduct thorough investigations and gather evidence regarding violations confirmed through investigation. Where
relevant issues arise, assistance shall be provided by the respective functional departments. Should significant violations,
disciplinary offences, or illegal acts be discovered, these must be reported to the Legal Department for joint investigation
and handling. The Company’s disciplinary measures encompass verbal warnings, written warnings, compensation,
demotion with salary reduction, dismissal, voluntary resignation, termination of employment contracts, and referral to
judicial authorities.
