3.     Committing to Green Development

The United Nations Sustainable Development Goals (UN SDGs) relevant to this chapter:




Guided by the principle that ‘clear waters and lush mountains are invaluable assets’, and amidst the global trend of
energy transformation, the Company is committed to a mission of green development. We are dedicated to advancing
the research and application of integrated solar power, energy storage, and charging solutions, as well as electrified
equipment. Pivoting on photovoltaic power generation, energy storage equipment, and charging infrastructure, we
will promote the widespread application of clean energy, supporting our clients in their low-carbon transition. We are
committed to collaborating with our global partners to protect the natural environment and establish green principles as the
cornerstone of high-quality development.

During the Reporting Period, the Company strictly complied with all applicable laws and regulations detailed in Appendix 4
– ‘Principal Laws and Regulations for Compliance’. No instances of material non-compliance were identified in relation to
legislation governing air and greenhouse gas emissions, discharges to water and land, or the generation of hazardous and
non-hazardous waste that would have a significant impact on the Company. Furthermore, during the Reporting Period, no
significant fines or other non-monetary penalties were imposed on the Company for non-compliance with relevant laws and
regulations.

      3.1 Environmental Management System

      The Company recognises that a systematic approach to environmental management is the cornerstone of achieving
      sustainable development. During the Reporting Period, we successfully obtained ISO 14001:2015 certification for our
      Environmental Management System, marking a significant milestone in our systematic approach to environmental
      management. This system is led by the Environment, Health and Safety (EHS) Department, which holds comprehensive
      responsibility for identifying, assessing and managing potential environmental hazards. It aims to establish and operate
      an environmental management model that adheres to international standards, thereby enhancing the Company’s overall
      environmental performance.




                                 Our ISO 14001:2015 Environmental Management System Certification


      3.2 Emissions and Waste Management

      Non-hazardous Waste Management

      The Company’s principal non-hazardous waste comprises general domestic refuse, as well as cardboard packaging and
      wooden pallets from materials. To ensure such waste is handled appropriately, we have adopted measures for segregated
      recycling and centralised management. The Company has standardised the provision of waste segregation facilities,
      placing separate bins for dry, wet, recyclable, and hazardous waste in areas such as lift lobbies and refuse rooms. All
      waste is handled by a government-accredited third party, with disposal methods that comply with all applicable laws and
      regulations.

      Furthermore, the Company actively promotes green office practices, advocating for a paperless office, encouraging the use
      of digital applications, issuing internal notices and plans electronically, and utilising double-sided printing to reduce waste
      generation at source.

      During the Reporting Period, over 99% of the Group’s non-hazardous waste was recycled or disposed of by qualified
      third-party contractors.
                                                                   2025 ANNUAL REPORT       BRETON TECHNOLOGY CO., LTD.       101




9. 2025 ENVIRONMENTAL, SOCIAL AND GOVERNANCE REPORT



Hazardous Waste Management

The hazardous waste generated by the Company primarily arises from the manufacturing process and mainly includes
waste oils, packaging drums, and paint residue.

•      Regulated Storage Management: The Company maintains a dedicated storage facility for hazardous chemical
       waste, ensuring centralised and compliant storage. The storage facility’s location adheres to national environmental
       standards and is equipped with safety measures including impermeable linings, leak prevention systems,
       weatherproofing, and ventilation. All hazardous waste is stored in specialised containers. Both the containers and
       storage areas are clearly labelled with details of the substance, its associated hazards, emergency procedures, and
       weight, thereby effectively mitigating environmental risks.

•      Full-Process Traceability: The Company has implemented a comprehensive hazardous waste ledger system.
       This system meticulously records every stage, from generation and warehousing to storage, removal, and transfer,
       enabling full life-cycle traceability.

•      Compliant disposal: Once a specified quantity is accumulated, the waste is transferred in its entirety to a
       government-authorised professional agency, holding the relevant qualifications, for transportation and processing.
       This process is subject to periodic inspections by regulatory bodies.

During the Reporting Period, 100% of the Group’s hazardous waste was compliantly disposed of by a qualified third party.

Given our low waste generation, we have not set specific quantitative waste reduction targets. Our hazardous waste is
effectively managed through centralised storage, spill prevention and professional third-party disposal; Our non-hazardous
waste volume was zero during the Reporting Period. Going forward, we will continue to refine waste data statistical scope
and accounting methodologies to improve accuracy, and will consider setting more specific reduction targets where
appropriate.

Emissions Management

The Company’s primary sources of waste gas emissions include processes such as paint coating and spraying, flue gas
from drying tunnels, and welding fumes. The Company has engaged a qualified, professional environmental equipment
provider responsible for the design, manufacture, installation, and commissioning of the treatment facilities for paint
emissions, drying emissions, and polishing dust. The Company strictly adheres to operational standards for the treatment
of gaseous emissions during its production processes, ensuring that all such emissions comply with national and local
environmental protection requirements.
102   BRETON TECHNOLOGY CO., LTD.       2025 ANNUAL REPORT




      9. 2025 ENVIRONMENTAL, SOCIAL AND GOVERNANCE REPORT


      System-Specific Precision Treatment:

      •      Paint Fume Treatment System: This system utilises a ‘pre-treatment, adsorption and concentration, and catalytic
             combustion’ process.

      •      Drying Exhaust Treatment System: This system utilises a ‘direct combustion and waste heat recovery’ process.

      •      Polishing Dust Treatment System: This system utilises a ‘bag filter dust collector and ventilation fan’ process.

      During the Reporting Period, the treatment rate of the Company’s exhaust gas facilities exceeded 95%.

      Wastewater and Noise Management

      Domestic sewage generated by the Company is discharged into the municipal sewage network for centralised treatment
      by the local water authority. In respect of noise management, noise control is incorporated as a technical evaluation
      criterion in our equipment procurement process. Furthermore, we implement measures such as sound insulation and
      vibration damping for principal noise sources to mitigate their impact on the surrounding environment.

      3.3 Energy and Resource Management

      Improving the efficiency of energy and resource utilisation is a key strategy for the business to address climate change
      and reduce operational costs. The Group continuously enhances its energy management system. We have formulated an
      energy conservation and emissions reduction plan and a preparatory plan for ISO 50001 certification. These plans clarify
      energy management responsibilities, standardise procedures for energy metering and energy-saving renovations, and
      implement a reward and penalty mechanism for energy conservation.

      The Company assigns dedicated personnel to be responsible for the statistical analysis of water and electricity
      consumption. They regularly monitor changes in energy consumption, formulate safety measures for electricity usage,
      and enhance the detailed management of energy utilisation. We periodically conduct campaigns to promote water
      conservation. Water-saving reminders are displayed at water points in office areas, such as washrooms and pantries,
      and we have comprehensively installed automatic sensor taps to reduce water wastage at the source. Concurrently, we
      conduct regular leakage detection for water supply facilities and concealed pipework, and enhance routine inspections and
      maintenance to eliminate water resource losses caused by ageing infrastructure or operational oversights. Furthermore, we
      require employees to turn off electronic equipment, air conditioning, and lighting systems when not in use. Air conditioning
      filters are cleaned and maintained on a regular basis to substantially improve operational energy efficiency, thereby driving
      the effective implementation of our green office initiatives through refined management practices.
                                                                   2025 ANNUAL REPORT       BRETON TECHNOLOGY CO., LTD.   103

We embed green principles throughout the construction and daily operations of our plants, demonstrating our commitment

      3.5 Green and Low-Carbon Products and Services

      In the era of carbon neutrality and intelligentisation, the Company addresses user needs by leveraging our leading
      electric products and comprehensive technological development capabilities. We are building an ecosystem structured
      around ‘photovoltaic storage microgrid power supply’, ‘new energy excavation, loading, and transportation equipment
      for mining engineering’, and ‘automated collaborative operations’. This enables us to provide clients with integrated
      solutions encompassing new energy power services, the sale and leasing of electric excavation and transport equipment,
      and intelligent excavation and transport services, with the aim of becoming a leading global power producer and mining
      services provider.

      Our construction machinery is powered entirely by non-fossil fuel sources, achieving zero exhaust emissions. Our electric
      loader and wide-body dump trucks offer a significant reduction in carbon emissions compared to their fuel-powered
      counterparts, presenting a more environmentally friendly alternative to traditional machinery. For example, over a five-year
      service life, a five-tonne class electric loader is projected to reduce carbon emissions by approximately 342.0 tonnes per
      unit. Similarly, over the same period, a 105-tonne class electric wide-body dump truck is projected to reduce carbon
      emissions by approximately 490.4 tonnes per unit during heavy-load uphill operations and by approximately 624.6
      tonnes per unit during heavy-load downhill operations. Compared to diesel equipment in the same class, each Breton
      Electric Loader can save clients approximately RMB350,000 in operating costs and reduce carbon dioxide emissions by
      approximately 180 tonnes annually; Each Breton pure electric mining truck can save clients approximately RMB700,000 in
      operating costs and reduce carbon dioxide emissions by approximately 400 tonnes annually.

      Regarding the circular economy, we incorporate its principles into our corporate operations, in accordance with the
      ‘Interim Measures for the Management of Recycling and Comprehensive Utilisation of Waste Power Batteries from New
      Energy Vehicles’. The Company has entered into a strategic partnership with a specialist recycling agency to establish
      a foundational power battery recycling system. We are committed to creating a complete, closed-loop industrial chain
      encompassing battery production, vehicle manufacturing, battery recycling, cascade utilisation, and regeneration and
      reuse. By leveraging our partners’ network of sites, we are establishing centralised storage points to build an extensive
      battery recycling network. This ensures that end-of-life power batteries are processed and recycled in a timely and
      compliant manner, and we are actively developing recycling channels for decommissioned batteries overseas.

      With regard to core technology research and development, we have made sustained investments in the independent
      development of key technologies, including pure electric drive powertrains, high energy-density power batteries, coaxial
      dual motors, and highly integrated multi-in-one controllers. Furthermore, through lightweight vehicle design and system
      optimisation, we continuously reduce resource consumption and carbon emissions throughout the entire product life cycle.
      Breton’s innovative applications, such as the automatic start-stop technology for power steering pumps and the intelligent
      strategy for the battery thermal management system, are tangible examples of our commitment to green and low-carbon
      principles in every detail of our operations.
                                                                 2025 ANNUAL REPORT      BRETON TECHNOLOGY CO., LTD.    105




9. 2025 ENVIRONMENTAL, SOCIAL AND GOVERNANCE REPORT



 Case Study:         The practical implementation of a systematic solution


 In our project in Abag Banner, Inner Mongolia, we successfully implemented an integrated, zero-carbon mining
 solution combining ‘photovoltaic power + electric mining trucks + autonomous driving’, setting a paradigm for
 green transformation within the industry. This project fully embodies the core values of our green and low-carbon
 products and services:

 •      In terms of clean energy supply, the mining area utilises idle land to construct a photovoltaic power
        generation system. This provides a low-cost, zero-emission source of clean electricity for our pure electric
        mining trucks, thereby creating a green, closed-loop system for energy production and consumption. This
        integrated model, which combines solar power generation, energy storage, charging, and application,
        fundamentally reduces reliance on fossil fuels.

 •      In the application of electrified products, the Group’s BRT105E all-electric mining truck has demonstrated
        outstanding energy efficiency during operation. According to operational data from the mining site, the
        vehicle’s average electricity consumption under heavy-load, uphill conditions is approximately 10 kWh
        per kilometre. Factoring in an electricity tariff of RMB0.47 per kWh, the operational cost is just RMB5 per
        kilometre. By contrast, a conventional diesel-powered mining truck under identical operating conditions
        consumes over 3 litres of fuel per kilometre, resulting in fuel costs exceeding RMB20. Based on an annual
        operation of 50,000 kilometres per vehicle at the mine site, each battery-electric mining truck can reduce
        annual operating costs by over RMB750,000, whilst also achieving a substantial reduction in carbon
        emissions.

 •      In terms of intelligent technology enablement, the Group has deployed 10 of its autonomous electric mining
        trucks into routine operation at the mine site, achieving an overall operational efficiency of approximately
        80% compared to manned vehicles. Crucially, the application of autonomous driving technology has
        markedly improved operational safety. Whereas the conventional model requires 20 drivers for 10 mining
        trucks, the same workload can now be managed by just 5 operations personnel. This not only lowers labour
        costs but also minimises the time staff are exposed to complex, high-risk environments, thereby delivering a
        dual improvement in both safety and efficiency.

 The Group provides not merely isolated pieces of electric equipment, but rather a comprehensive, full-scope
 solution encompassing green energy supply, electrified transportation, and intelligent dispatching. This solution
 creates threefold value for customers: at the economic level, the combination of ‘Photovoltaic power generation +
 electric mining trucks’ substantially reduces long-term operating costs; at the environmental level, it achieves the
 deep decarbonisation of mining operations, robustly supporting global emissions reduction targets.

      3.6 Response to Climate Change

      The Group is acutely aware of the far-reaching impact of climate change on our business and societal development, and
      has taken numerous actions to address this global challenge. In active support of the national ‘Dual Carbon’ strategy,
      we reduce greenhouse gas emissions throughout the operations of the Company and its value chain by managing
      climate-related risks and opportunities and implementing energy-saving and carbon-reduction measures, thereby
      enhancing our resilience to climate risk.

      We manage climate change risks in accordance with the disclosure recommendations in Part D: Climate-related
      Disclosures of the ESG Code. Our management and disclosures cover four key areas: governance, strategy, risk
      management, and metrics and targets, with the aim of mitigating the adverse effects of climate change on the Company.

      Governance

      The Board is the Company’s highest governing body for ESG matters and has overall responsibility for the management
      of ESG-related risks and opportunities, including those related to climate change. The Company has established a
      three-tiered ESG management structure authorised by the Board, comprising an ESG Committee, an ESG Office, and an
      ESG Working Group. For further details, please refer to Section 2.1, ‘Sustainable Governance’.

      The Board is formally briefed on ESG matters at least annually to maintain oversight of the Company’s overall ESG
      performance and risk profile, including climate-related issues. As the standing supervisory body, the ESG Committee
      convenes at least quarterly to systematically review the progress in the identification, assessment, and management
      of climate-related risks and opportunities. Furthermore, the ESG Committee will continue to monitor progress towards
      achieving these targets, and will regularly review the relevant performance data and analytical reports compiled by the ESG
      Office. In respect of material climate-related matters, such as the actual impact of extreme weather events on production
      and operations or the need for strategic adjustments necessitated by significant changes in climate policy, the ESG
      Working Group will report promptly to the ESG Committee and the Board through a dedicated reporting mechanism. This
      ensures that the governance level is kept abreast of the latest developments and can make timely decisions.
                                                                   2025 ANNUAL REPORT      BRETON TECHNOLOGY CO., LTD.       107




9. 2025 ENVIRONMENTAL, SOCIAL AND GOVERNANCE REPORT


The Board and the ESG Committee consider climate-related risks and opportunities as a key factor when overseeing the
formulation and execution of the company’s strategy, major transaction decisions, risk management procedures, and
related policies. The conclusions of the assessment of climate-related risks and opportunities serve as an important basis
for decision-making, ensuring that the strategic direction is aligned with global sustainable development trends.

The Company plans to organise specialised climate risk training for members of the Board and senior management,
covering the latest policy developments, scenario analysis, transition risks, and physical risks, to ensure that oversight
capabilities remain in step with global regulatory trends. Currently, the Company has not directly incorporated
climate-related performance indicators into the remuneration assessments for its senior executives. However, an evaluation
has commenced to assess the feasibility of their gradual inclusion in the future, thereby strengthening the alignment of
incentives to drive the achievement of our climate objectives.

Strategy

The Company has identified climate-related physical and transitional risks, and is exploring the corresponding potential
opportunities. We recognise that these climate risks may have a material impact on the Company in the short term (within
3 years), medium term (3-5 years), and long term (over 5 years), encompassing both physical environmental changes and
the challenges presented by the transition to a low-carbon economy. Based on the nature of the Company’s business
and its operational characteristics, we analyse the associated risks and opportunities and formulate targeted mitigation
measures.

To address the aforementioned Climate-related risks and opportunities, the Group has strategically established ‘new
energy + intelligent technology’ as its long-term development direction and has set phased targets for the promotion of
its electric products and solutions. In terms of our business model, we lower the initial investment threshold for customers
and accelerate market penetration through innovative approaches such as equipment leasing and energy management
services. At an operational level, we integrate climate adaptation considerations, such as flood prevention and heat
dissipation, into our project designs and establish contingency plans to enhance our business resilience.
114   BRETON TECHNOLOGY CO., LTD.       2025 ANNUAL REPORT




      9. 2025 ENVIRONMENTAL, SOCIAL AND GOVERNANCE REPORT


      •      Adjustment of Product Structure: We will continue to increase investment in research and development and
             production resources for pure electric construction machinery, progressively raising the proportion of electric
             products within our overall business portfolio. We plan to launch a new generation of mining trucks, Electric
             Loaders, electric excavators, and mobile charging vehicles in succession.

      •      Clean Energy Strategy: Transitioning from a dedicated equipment manufacturer to a system solutions provider of
             ‘Green Energy + Electric Equipment’. We have partnered with Boyue New Energy to construct rooftop photovoltaic
             systems at our own factories and are promoting a ‘Photovoltaics + Electric Mining Trucks’ model to clients,
             enabling direct participation in the generation and application of clean energy.

      •      Intelligent Upgrades: Integrating autonomous driving technology with electrified products to enhance product
             value-add and customer value, whilst reducing the need for manual labour in high-risk positions.

      The Company recognises the importance of scenario analysis in assessing strategy resilience but has not yet completed
      formal climate-related scenario analysis. The Company is developing internal capabilities and gathering the required data
      to conduct reliable scenario modelling. Accordingly, the Company has applied the Capabilities Relief and Reasonable
      Information Relief.

      Given the Company’s scale and business nature, both current and anticipated financial effects are not material, no
      significant financial impacts have been quantified, and none are currently difficult to quantify reliably in a manner that
      would alter strategic decisions. The Company has therefore applied the Financial Effects Relief and the Capabilities Relief.
      The Company’s existing sustainability initiatives do not constitute a specific climate-related transition plan at this stage.
      The Company will continue to assess the need for a formal transition plan in future reporting periods as climate-related
      requirements and internal capabilities evolve.

      Risk Management

      In terms of risk management, the Company is in the process of integrating climate-related risks into its overall risk
      management framework, to be identified, assessed, and monitored through standardised processes.

      •      Risk Identification

             Input data includes: developments in national and international climate policies and regulations, internal operational
             data, supplier information, customer feedback, and the results of stakeholder engagement. The scope of the risk
             assessment covers the Group’s headquarters, all manufacturing plants and principal functional departments.

      •      Risk Assessment

             The Company assesses climate-related risk based on two dimensions: the likelihood of its occurrence and the
             severity of its impact. The factors considered for the severity of impact include the potential increase in operating
             costs, the potential effect on revenue, the impact on asset values, the risk to employee safety, and the effect on
             the Company’s reputation. The assessment process spans the entire business chain, from product research and
             development and the construction of energy systems to the delivery of complete solutions.
                                                                      2025 ANNUAL REPORT        BRETON TECHNOLOGY CO., LTD.        115




9. 2025 ENVIRONMENTAL, SOCIAL AND GOVERNANCE REPORT


•      Risk Prioritisation

       Following an assessment of the nature, likelihood, and magnitude of climate-related risks, the Company
       incorporates these into its risk map alongside other business risks (such as market, credit, and operational risks) for
       prioritisation. Material risks are given priority for inclusion in the risk register, and management responsibilities are
       duly assigned.

•      Risk Monitoring

       The Company, through its ESG Working Group, regularly tracks climate policy and market dynamics; the Production
       and Operations Department monitors for any abnormal fluctuations in energy consumption and emissions data on
       a monthly basis; and the EHS department supervises the implementation and drilling of emergency response plans
       for climate-related risks.

Metrics and Targets

With respect to the cross-industry indicators, the Company has applied a Reasonable Information Relief. The Company
does not currently maintain or employ an internal carbon pricing mechanism in its decision-making processes. In view
of the low materiality of climate-related risks and the limited carbon footprint of its operations, the Company does not
consider it necessary to adopt an internal carbon pricing mechanism at this stage. The Company will progressively explore
the disclosure of other relevant industry-specific metrics in future Reporting Periods, in line with its business operations.

During the Reporting Period, the Company’s capital expenditure, financing, or investment concerning climate-related risks
and opportunities were primarily concentrated in the following areas:

During the Reporting Period, direct greenhouse gas (GHG) emissions (Scope 1) were principally derived from road

processes.

      To systemically advance our decarbonisation work and align with the national ‘Dual Carbon’ strategy, the Company has
      established short-, medium-, and long-term climate-related targets to manage greenhouse gas emissions and promote
      sustainable development in a structured manner. Our short-term targets (by 2030) focus on strengthening our data
      infrastructure, with a commitment to enhancing internal data collection systems and undertaking a systematic accounting
      of Scope 3 emissions.

      Our medium-term targets (by 2035) centre on transforming our energy mix, with the objective of ensuring that 50% of the
      annual electricity consumption at our manufacturing plants is sourced from photovoltaic power generation systems or other
      renewable energy. This target applies to all manufacturing plants within the Group, with the proportion of renewable energy
      used (defined as the percentage of electricity from renewable sources out of total electricity consumption) serving as the
      core metric. Furthermore, with 2024 as our baseline year, we aim to reduce our Scope 1 and Scope 2 emissions by 20%
      from the baseline level by 2035.

      Our long-term goal is to achieve carbon neutrality by 2055, thereby aligning with the national ‘dual carbon’ objectives and
      fully honouring our corporate social responsibility in response to climate change.

      In relation to the setting, review, and monitoring of our objectives, the methodology for the Company’s short-term targets
      follows the Greenhouse Gas Protocol to ensure a scientific basis for our Scope 3 inventory method; our long-term targets
      are aligned with national strategies. The ESG Committee will review progress against these targets annually and assess
      their appropriateness. The metrics used to monitor progress include total GHG emissions and emission intensity. Should
      the targets require revision due to material business adjustments or changes in the external regulatory environment, such
      revisions and the corresponding reasons will be disclosed in the ESG report following review and approval by the Board.
      At present, our greenhouse gas emissions targets have not been independently verified by a third party.

      4.1 Technology Ethics and Digital and Intelligent Transformation

      We place paramount importance on technology ethics, actively fulfilling our social responsibilities by integrating ethical
      principles into every facet of R&D, production, and operations. We relentlessly advance technology for the greater good
      of humanity. Prior to product development and technological deployment, the Group conducts comprehensive ethical
      risk assessments to identify potential hazards and formulate countermeasures, ensuring all applications align with ethical
      standards. Throughout 2025, the Group recorded no incidents of technology ethics violations.

      The Group actively advances the establishment of digital platforms and systems. This year saw the completion of upgrades
      to the Product Lifecycle Management (PLM) system and Enterprise Resource Planning (ERP) system, alongside the
      development of systems tailored to company processes based on core business logic. This enables digital empowerment
      across human resources, finance, dual carbon management, and smart manufacturing management.

      Case Study: Breton’s Autonomous Mining Trucks and Loaders

      The Company is leveraging technological innovations in embodied intelligence to transform autonomous vehicles into
      powerful robots, fundamentally reshaping mining production logic. This technological breakthrough challenges the paradox
      that “mining equals destruction,” forging a new equilibrium between industrial civilisation and ecological civilisation through
      the interplay of code and machinery.

      The massive steel chassis transcends mere transport, deeply integrating with digital sensors like LiDAR and cameras
      within an embodied intelligence framework to form a distributed perception network. Through direct mechanical interaction
      with ore, slopes, and depressions, it generates real-time action strategies. This paradigm of ‘body as intelligence’ evolves
      machines from passive puppets executing commands into autonomous entities capable of adapting to unstructured
      environments.

      By deeply integrating the innate physical responses of mechanical systems with AI models, Breton’s unmanned equipment
      achieves a multidimensional deconstruction of the mining environment: they not only “see” the geometric contours of the
      terrain but also “sense” the mechanical properties of the ground; they not only “recognise” the visual characteristics of
      ore but also “understand” the physical essence of the ore layer. This perceptual capability, transcending traditional sensor
      logic, represents the unique evolutionary trajectory embodied intelligence bestows upon engineering machinery.

      Confronted with the complexity of unstructured mining environments, Breton’s autonomous equipment develops a
      distinctive “physical intuition” through mechanical interactions between its chassis and surroundings: when tyres traverse
      loose rock strata, the drive system’s torque distribution automatically optimises based on ground feedback, much like
      a mountaineer adjusting stride for shifting scree; When the loader bucket contacts ore, hydraulic pressure feedback
      dynamically corrects the digging angle, preventing equipment wear caused by rigid operations in conventional machinery.
      This capability stems not from complex navigation algorithms, but from deep coupling between mechanical structures and
      physical laws, transforming every action into an instantaneous experiment in environmental adaptation.
                                                                      2025 ANNUAL REPORT       BRETON TECHNOLOGY CO., LTD.        119




9. 2025 ENVIRONMENTAL, SOCIAL AND GOVERNANCE REPORT



Case Study: Breton Enhances Mining Equipment Reliability Through Full-Chain Intelligence

The electrical system serves as the ‘nervous system’ of mining equipment; any malfunction will bring the entire vehicle to a
standstill. Our company has introduced intelligent low-voltage distribution units to replace traditional fuse modules. These
units monitor load operating conditions in real time, meet over-temperature and over-current protection requirements, and
can self-reset after fault clearance without manual intervention, thereby reducing maintenance workload.

Regarding battery management, the intelligent thermal management system adjusts cooling power based on real-time
temperatures, maintaining batteries within their optimal operating range. Intelligent charge balancing automatically regulates
current according to voltage differentials across multiple battery cells, enabling low-current equalisation maintenance to
reduce battery degradation. These features ensure stable battery system operation under high-intensity conditions, as
evidenced by Breton’s pure-electric mining trucks retaining 88% capacity after five years of service.

The company’s competitive edge lies in its integrated vehicle intelligence design capabilities. The proprietary Vehicle Control
Unit (VCU), acting as the central brain, integrates real-time vehicle status, load, driver commands and environmental data,
dynamically coordinating core components such as the battery management system and motor controller. It intelligently
adjusts battery output during heavy uphill climbs and optimises energy recovery rhythms on descents, ensuring responsive
power delivery while preventing current surges to extend battery lifespan. The VCU transmits operational data in real-time
to the customer management platform, where algorithms generate precise maintenance recommendations. This empowers
maintenance teams to make data-driven decisions and schedule servicing proactively, thereby reducing unexpected failure
rates.

Taking the BRT968K loader as an example, its transmission oil pump employs a dual-function intelligent control unit that
proactively increases lubricant supply based on operational demands. This reduces metal wear and extends transmission
service life. The introduction of ADAS (Advanced Driver Assistance Systems) significantly enhances the safety performance
of our intelligent loaders. These features encompass forward collision warning, rear-view camera or 360° surround-view
systems, and cab occupant monitoring capabilities. This enables real-time assessment of the driver’s condition, preventing
fatigue driving or distracted operation. Concurrently, the vehicle positioning system and communication/dispatch system
enable precise equipment management. Tyre Pressure Monitoring System (TPMS) and Blind Spot Detection (BSD) further
enhance driving safety, thereby reducing the occurrence of accidents.

The company maintains dedicated autonomous driving and vehicle development teams, incorporating automated
operations into design considerations from the product initiation stage to achieve synchronised hardware and software
iteration. Consequently, our vehicles have become the preferred partner for an increasing number of autonomous driving
enterprises, research institutions, and mining technology departments.
120   BRETON TECHNOLOGY CO., LTD.       2025 ANNUAL REPORT




      9. 2025 ENVIRONMENTAL, SOCIAL AND GOVERNANCE REPORT



      4.2 Product Quality and Safety

      The Group employs advanced innovative technologies alongside traditional craftsmanship to deliver aesthetically refined
      products with outstanding performance. We recognise that product quality responsibility extends throughout the entire
      value chain – from design and manufacturing to delivery and usage – and remain steadfast in our commitment to product
      quality and customer service.

      Product Quality Management

      The Group has established a quality management system covering the entire product lifecycle, implementing stringent
      controls at every stage from conceptual design to market launch. Through the synergistic application of quality planning,
      quality control, quality improvement, and quality assurance, we achieve rigorous oversight across all phases of the product
      lifecycle. A dynamic management mechanism based on product quality and safety risk prevention and monitoring has been
      established, overseeing the entire process from R&D, material procurement, production processes, to shipment. Quality
      control throughout the production process encompasses material inspection, in-process inspection, final vehicle inspection
      upon completion, and final vehicle inspection prior to dispatch. Annual internal and external quality system audits are
      conducted.

      1.     Establish component incoming inspection standards and conduct incoming inspections on parts;

      2.     Verify production processes based on critical control points during assembly;

      3.     Final vehicle inspections upon assembly completion and prior to factory dispatch must be conducted in accordance
             with inspection standards. Inspection items include: static testing, road testing, figure-eight circuit testing, ramp
             testing, jacking hydraulic pressure testing, braking distance testing, three-phase comprehensive testing, and
             charging testing;

      4.     Battery cells shall undergo mandatory inspection by a third-party organisation in accordance with national
             standards.

      5.     The complete vehicle shall undergo type approval testing by a third-party organisation.
                                                                      2025 ANNUAL REPORT        BRETON TECHNOLOGY CO., LTD.        121




9. 2025 ENVIRONMENTAL, SOCIAL AND GOVERNANCE REPORT



Product After-Sales Service Management

Product after-sales service management is strictly implemented in accordance with the Finished Product Inspection
Specifications, encompassing post-production line inspection and factory exit inspection. Inspections include static
testing, road testing, figure-eight circuit testing, slope testing, hydraulic lift testing, braking distance testing, three-item
comprehensive testing, and charging testing.

Safety remains our paramount concern, ensuring users are not exposed to safety risks due to immature technology.
We have established a Safety Committee, adhering to the safety principle of “Safety First, Prevention Primarily”. We
have established a robust product quality and safety accountability system, committed to driving continuous innovation
in product safety technology, enhancing monitoring platform efficiency, optimising after-sales service capabilities,
strengthening incident response and handling, and comprehensively safeguarding product and user safety.

The Group has established policies and standard procedures for handling complaints submitted through various channels
(such as telephone, email, and WeChat) from retail outlets across different countries. During the reporting period, the
Group received 27 complaints from clients, 100% of complaints were related to equipment and parts malfunctions, and all
of them have been resolved by replacing the equipment or sending personnel to repair them. Our customer service teams
meticulously document and address all customer complaints, investigating underlying causes, following up on issues, and
providing resolutions. Dedicated customer service teams operate in each region, supported by a 400 service hotline. Our
customer service department operates 24 hours online to address product quality and repair issues, safeguarding our ESG
reputation and upholding sustainable development. We meticulously document the key points of customer complaints,
investigate underlying causes, and propose resolutions.
122   BRETON TECHNOLOGY CO., LTD.   2025 ANNUAL REPORT




      9. 2025 ENVIRONMENTAL, SOCIAL AND GOVERNANCE REPORT



      ISO Certification

      The company holds the GB/T 19001-2016/ISO 9001:2015 Quality Management System certification, with the scope of
      certification extending to subsidiaries including Breton Shandong and Breton Wuhan.

4.3 Research, Development and Innovation

Upholding the core value of “pioneering innovation”, the Group adheres to the transformation and upgrading strategy
of “high-end, digital-intelligent, and green development”. It has established a technological innovation system and a
comprehensive institutional management framework, formulating the Group’s innovation strategy and technological
roadmap to steer innovative advancement. The Group’s Chief Technology Officer is responsible for determining the
strategic direction of the Group’s technological development, providing advisory opinions and basis for decisions on major
technological activities. The Group’s Technology Committee fully leverages and enhances the technical influence of experts
to improve the Group’s technological decision-making system.

During the reporting period, we have continuously researched and developed 21 projects, resulting in the launch of 10 new
products.

The Company has established a systematic and institutionalised R&D and innovation management system to ensure the
effective implementation of technological innovation activities and maximise resource efficiency. The company operates a
Central Research Institute as the centralised R&D management department, responsible for coordinating all corporate R&D
activities and implementing end-to-end process control covering project initiation, design development, verification and
validation, through to commercialisation. R&D projects are managed through a tiered system (A-D grades) and a project
manager accountability framework to ensure clear objectives and defined responsibilities.

Regarding innovation mechanisms, the company encourages comprehensive R&D spanning fundamental technological
breakthroughs to product optimisation and refinement. Through cross-departmental reviews, phased acceptance
assessments, and regular evaluations, the quality and efficiency of R&D are continuously enhanced. Regarding R&D
expenditure, the company implements dedicated budget management. The Finance Department conducts independent
accounting for each project, standardising fund usage and allocation. Development expenditures meeting capitalisation
criteria are capitalised to ensure sustained investment in R&D resources.

4.4 Intellectual Property Protection

The Group places high importance on intellectual property protection, having established a comprehensive system
covering the entire process from patent applications and technical secrecy to unified management of outcomes. This
ensures effective protection and conversion of innovative achievements. Concurrently, through performance assessments
of R&D personnel and project-specific incentive measures, the Group stimulates the innovative enthusiasm of technical
staff, fostering an organisational environment conducive to long-term innovation.

The Group actively safeguards intellectual property rights, including product patents, trademarks, core technologies,
modes of use, and packaging designs. During the reporting period, the Group secured 12 newly authorised patents in
China, bringing its cumulative total of authorised patents in China to 163. Overseas, three new patents were authorised,
maintaining a cumulative total of three authorised patents. No reports or complaints regarding serious violations of
intellectual property laws and regulations were received by the Group during this period. The Group conducted three
intellectual property protection training sessions, covering 90% of its employees.
124   BRETON TECHNOLOGY CO., LTD.         2025 ANNUAL REPORT




      9. 2025 ENVIRONMENTAL, SOCIAL AND GOVERNANCE REPORT



      4.5 Privacy Protection and Information Security

      The Group strictly adheres to the principal legal and regulatory requirements listed in the Appendix, ensuring that personnel
      engaged in technological activities possess relevant qualifications and that research facilities and infrastructure comply with
      stipulated standards. The processing of personal data, including personal privacy data and biometric information, must
      conform to relevant personal information protection regulations. Activities such as data collection, storage, processing,
      and utilisation, as well as the development of new technologies for research and development data, must also comply with
      national data security and personal information protection regulations.

      At the technical level, we employ opt-in consent and/or client-confirmed authorisation for data collection, with encrypted
      storage to guarantee data subjects’ rights to information, access, rectification, erasure, and restriction of processing.
      Measures, including information system access controls, network access and login restrictions, outgoing document audits,
      keyword identification, and screen watermarking, enhance outgoing information management to prevent privacy breaches.

      To further enhance information security management, the Company has established a comprehensive prevention and
      control mechanism, encouraging any employee to immediately report actual or potential information security incidents to
      the Legal Department via email, ensuring prompt response and resolution. During the reporting period, the Group received
      no reports or complaints regarding serious breaches of privacy protection and information security laws and regulations.

      Privacy Reporting Channel

      Reporting email: legal@breton.top

      4.6 Responsible Supply Chain

      The Group’s ability to deliver high-quality products and services to customers in a timely manner is underpinned by a robust
      and resilient supply chain. The Group has established a comprehensive supplier management system, encompassing a
      series of regulations and procedures including the Supplier Management Procedures and the Breton Technology Co., Ltd.
      Procurement Management System. These processes and regulations are designed to comprehensively control supplier
      product quality, supply stability, and service consistency, thereby ensuring the Group’s products and services maintain
      consistently high standards.
                                                                     2025 ANNUAL REPORT       BRETON TECHNOLOGY CO., LTD.        125




9. 2025 ENVIRONMENTAL, SOCIAL AND GOVERNANCE REPORT


The Procurement Department is responsible for executing relevant agreements with suppliers, including the General
Terms and Conditions of Purchase Contract and Confidentiality Agreements. The Quality Department handles the signing
of Quality Agreements with suppliers, while the Technical Department executes Technical Agreements with suppliers as
required.

Procurement personnel initiate the Supplier Onboarding process within the system. For mass-production suppliers in
manufacturing, the procurement officer submits an application, which is reviewed by the Head of Procurement and the
Procurement Director, and becomes effective upon approval by the Chairman. For R&D projects or those requiring urgent
and rapid production, the procurement officer initiates the process, which is reviewed by the Head of Procurement and
approved by the Procurement Director. Upon approval, if no new product trial production is required, the supplier is added
to the approved supplier list. If new product trial production is necessary, the corresponding process is initiated via the OA
system. Following quality verification and confirmation, the supplier is included in the approved supplier list.

The Customer Service Department shall submit the collated post-sales issues via the OA system using the “Quality
Information Feedback and Rectification” process. Where the Quality Department issues a non-conformance report, the
Procurement Department shall notify the supplier of the non-conforming product details and require them to complete root
cause analysis and implement corrective actions within the stipulated timeframe.

For production materials deemed non-compliant by Quality, the Quality Department shall issue a determination notice
specifying the procurement liaison responsible for subsequent material tracking. The warehouse executes transfers based
on the Transfer Request; for replacements, the Procurement Department initiates a replacement request, while returns
require the Procurement Department to prepare a return note before transferring it to the warehouse, which processes the
Goods Receipt Note as a red-letter document.

To address supply chain risks, we have established a comprehensive supply chain assessment system employing a holistic
approach to identify suppliers’ environmental, human rights, and corporate governance risks. This encompasses internal
risk assessments, external environmental analysis, data and statistical analysis, due diligence, and risk control mechanisms.
Building upon this comprehensive initial risk identification, we have implemented a rigorous supplier admission mechanism
requiring suppliers to hold relevant certifications, including environmental certifications, to meet green and environmental
requirements. During the onboarding of new suppliers in the reporting period, we implemented a management process
encompassing integrity agreements, qualification documentation submission, review and evaluation, followed by supplier
rectification and re-evaluation. This integrates suppliers’ sustainability policies, practices, and performance into the
registration, assessment, and audit processes. Initial screening for new suppliers requires companies holding ISO9001 and
ISO/IATF16949 certifications to submit copies of their certificates. For companies without any quality system certification,
we assess our actual supplier resource requirements and, where necessary, provide guidance to enhance their quality
systems, mandating compliance with ISO9001 standards and subsequent certification.
126   BRETON TECHNOLOGY CO., LTD.       2025 ANNUAL REPORT




      9. 2025 ENVIRONMENTAL, SOCIAL AND GOVERNANCE REPORT


      We emphasise ethical and environmental standards, requiring third-party manufacturers to adhere to these principles
      throughout production to ensure sustainability compliance. Routine audits maintain performance benchmarks. We foster
      robust partnerships with suppliers committed to sustainability and possessing robust ESG frameworks. Notably, one of
      our key suppliers was recognised as one of the “Top Ten Chinese ESG Role Models” by China Media Group in 2023. This
      supplier has established clear carbon neutrality targets, aiming to achieve carbon neutrality for its core operations by 2025
      and across its entire value chain by 2035.

      Furthermore, given the bulk and weight of construction machinery, logistics inherently presents significant challenges
      and risks. Throughout our logistics operations, safety and environmental protection are prioritised. We collaborate with
      reputable, compliant third-party logistics providers to ensure adherence to transport regulations across all jurisdictions
      along the supply chain, thereby maintaining safety, reliability, and operational efficiency. To minimise environmental impact,
      we optimise transport routes to reduce carbon emissions and actively minimise packaging material usage. We select
      biodegradable and recyclable materials, such as paper cushioning and inflatable film, as alternatives to traditional plastic
      foam. We also encourage suppliers to adopt sustainable and environmentally friendly packaging solutions.

      During the reporting period, we will implement a comprehensive supplier quality training programme. Through diverse
      methods including technical exchanges and training sessions, we will engage in multi-dimensional dialogue with suppliers
      to ensure they fully grasp the Group’s rigorous standards regarding product technical characteristics, quality requirements,
      potential deviations, and safety and environmental considerations. This initiative aims to assist suppliers in enhancing
      their capabilities in product quality innovation, quality assurance, and technical services. This will strengthen supply chain
      resilience and reliability while effectively reducing operational costs, thereby further consolidating the Group’s market
      position and brand influence. The ultimate objective is to ensure products meet the most stringent quality standards from
      source to final delivery, realising our vision of mutual progress with suppliers.

      The Group also recognises the significance of environmental and social risks within supply chain activities, such as
      unforeseeable and unavoidable circumstances including extreme weather and public emergencies. The Group intends to
      establish additional ESG risk identification criteria and implement measures to monitor relevant supplier conduct, thereby
      addressing potential adverse environmental and social impacts.

5.     Fostering a Harmonious Workplace and Fulfilling Social Responsibility

United Nations Sustainable Development Goals (UNSDGs) addressed in this chapter




“The essence of governance lies in securing the right people.” Breton recognises that talent is the foremost resource for
corporate development. During the reporting period, we will implement a more open talent strategy, recruiting individuals
without prejudice, motivating contributors, and empowering strivers. As The Analects states: “Advance the upright and
set aside the crooked, then the people will submit. “Through equitable mechanisms, expansive platforms, and generous
rewards, we shall attract global talent, pool formidable strength, and jointly compose Breton’s glorious chapter.
128   BRETON TECHNOLOGY CO., LTD.       2025 ANNUAL REPORT




      9. 2025 ENVIRONMENTAL, SOCIAL AND GOVERNANCE REPORT



      5.1 Labour Compliance

      The Group strictly adheres to relevant International Labour Organisation conventions and the laws and regulations of the
      countries and regions where it operates. The employment of child labour and forced labour is strictly prohibited. During the
      reporting period, no instances of child labour or forced labour were identified within the Group.

      The Group strictly prohibits the employment of child labour and forced labour in any form. The Human Resources
      Department rigorously verifies the identity of new employees through multiple compliance procedures, including the
      collection and verification of original identity documents and onboarding registration forms. The Group explicitly prohibits
      the employment of child labour in its Staff Handbook. Relevant departments also regularly review employment records and
      data to ensure no instances of child labour or forced labour occur.

      5.2 Employment Details and Diversity

      Employee Details

      As of 31 December 2025, there were 334 employees across the Group’s operational locations, comprising 330 employees
      in Mainland China and 4 employees in Hong Kong. All employees within the operational locations are full-time. The
      male-to-female ratio among employees is approximately 85:15. For further details, please refer to the “Social Performance
      Data Table” section of this ESG Report.

      The Group strictly complies with employment-related laws and regulations in the regions where it operates. For details,
      please refer to Appendix 4 — “Key Applicable Laws and Regulations”. During the reporting period, the Group did not
      receive any reports or complaints regarding serious violations of employment-related laws and regulations. The Group
      supports diversity and equal development within its workforce and is committed to eliminating all forms of discrimination
      and harassment in the workplace. We place high value on team diversity, with members hailing from various ethnic
      backgrounds including Han, Kazakh, Hui, and Yi. We recruit employees with disabilities, assigning them specific tasks
      commensurate with their capabilities, thereby fulfilling our corporate social responsibility by empowering them to realise
      their full potential and enhance their quality of life.

      Staff Recruitment and Promotion

      We standardise key recruitment processes, including the submission of recruitment requirements, interview screening,
      hiring approvals, and probationary period management. This ensures fairness and transparency in recruitment
      management, laying a solid foundation for building a healthy and equitable working environment. We are committed to
      establishing diverse talent development pathways and maintaining transparent, fair promotion mechanisms. This provides
      employees with an equitable environment for advancement, ensuring that every employee’s efforts and talents are treated
      justly. We adhere to standardised employee management guidelines, covering recruitment, probationary-to-permanent
      conversion, resignation or dismissal procedures, and handover processes. Further details are outlined in our Recruitment
      Management System.
                                                                     2025 ANNUAL REPORT       BRETON TECHNOLOGY CO., LTD.        129




9. 2025 ENVIRONMENTAL, SOCIAL AND GOVERNANCE REPORT



Employee Turnover

We strictly adhere to labour contracts and relevant laws and regulations in the countries and regions where we operate
to establish employee departure procedures (including resignation and dismissal), safeguarding employee rights. Upon
an employee’s departure, we conduct exit interviews to understand their reasons for leaving and gather feedback for the
Group.

Total Employee Turnover Rate: 36.1%

5.3 Remuneration and Benefits

Employee Rights

The Group has established a comprehensive remuneration policy covering wages, bonuses, benefits and allowances,
aiming to provide fair and market-competitive total rewards to attract, motivate and retain talent. We ensure all employees,
regardless of gender, enjoy equal pay, benefits, and promotion opportunities, actively opposing discrimination. Generally,
remuneration is determined based on each employee’s qualifications, experience, position, and tenure. We strictly comply
with local laws and regulations where we operate, providing comprehensive statutory welfare protections.

We regularly update our Staff Handbook, which details leave entitlements, benefits, and rights. A trade union safeguards
employee interests. Additional benefits include meal allowances, travel subsidies, festive bonuses, health check-ups, and
other employee perks. We organise regular team-building activities and distribute statutory festive benefits to enhance
team cohesion and a sense of belonging. For instance, both Shandong Breton and Wuhan Breton within our Group
have entered into health examination service agreements with Meinian Health. Shandong Breton distributes peanut oil,
mooncakes, zongzi, and white flour as festive benefits to employees. In Shanghai, we distribute E-coupon as festive
benefits. Furthermore, we regularly conduct training courses to enhance employees’ comprehensive skills and capabilities.

5.4 Talent Development Planning

The Group fully recognises the importance of employees continuously enhancing their professional knowledge and skills
and has established a comprehensive employee development and training policy. We provide diverse internal and external
training opportunities for staff at all levels, including management personnel, sales and marketing staff, and operational and
back-office personnel. These encompass induction training, role-specific qualification training, professional knowledge and
business skills enhancement, comprehensive management training, and advanced development programmes.

During the reporting period, employees across all locations completed a total of 2,100 training hours, with an average of
approximately 6.5 hours per employee.
130   BRETON TECHNOLOGY CO., LTD.       2025 ANNUAL REPORT




      9. 2025 ENVIRONMENTAL, SOCIAL AND GOVERNANCE REPORT


      We have established a strategy-driven career development framework grounded in organisational strategy decoding and
      talent value creation. The management sequence focuses on enhancing organisational effectiveness, targeting role groups
      with team management responsibilities and resource allocation authority. Leadership competency models are employed
      to build talent pipelines. The professional sequence emphasises technical depth development, establishing specialist
      roles in core technical domains. Practitioners must possess systematic professional knowledge and the ability to tackle
      complex problems, driving business value growth through technological innovation. Group production site sequence talent
      development requires practitioners to possess practical experience in production operations management, accumulated
      expertise in audit certification, qualification and competency certification, and quality assurance management practice.

      5.5 Occupational Health and Safety

      The Group consistently prioritises occupational health and safety, establishing “Respect for Life, Safe Development” as its
      core guiding principle and steadfastly pursuing the ultimate goal of “zero accidents”. To this end, we have formulated and
      regularly update internal regulations including the Safety Management System, Safety Inspection Management System,
      Production Safety Operation Specifications, Assembly Operation Safety Specifications, Equipment Safety Operating
      Procedures, and Production Workshop On-site Management System. This ensures seamless and efficient operation at
      every level, from top-level design to grassroots implementation.

      The Group has established a Work Safety Leadership Team comprising principal officers from all company units, with the
      General Manager overseeing the organisation and leadership of overall work safety. Its primary responsibilities include
      exercising comprehensive leadership over the company’s work safety management, researching and formulating work
      safety measures and labour protection plans, inspecting and supervising production safety, and investigating and handling
      accidents.

      The Group has established a Safety Committee, which conducts random on-site inspections based on issues identified
      and rectification progress across workshops. Should violations or unresolved rectification items be discovered, responsible
      departments and personnel will face penalties in accordance with relevant regulations. The Safety Committee is chaired by
      the company’s Vice President, who bears overall responsibility for safety production management.

      We have established comprehensive accident emergency response and investigation procedures, clearly defining the
      processes and mechanisms for accident reporting, investigation, handling, and subsequent rectification. In the event of a
      safety or health incident, on-site personnel must immediately report to their department head, who shall promptly notify
      the Safety Committee. The Safety Committee organises relevant departments to form an investigation team, conducting a
      thorough inquiry into the cause and sequence of events to ensure effective implementation of corrective measures.

      Our employees are central to our business operations, and safeguarding their health and safety underpins our long-term
      success. We have established a robust health and safety protection system alongside an occupational health and safety
      risk management framework. Under the guidance of dedicated personnel, we conduct regular occupational safety
      education and maintain safety facilities. Our standardised safety inspection system incorporates routine oversight methods
      such as spot checks and patrols to monitor operational conditions in real time. We perform daily safety and environmental
      inspections, promptly rectifying any non-compliant issues.

      5.6 Social Responsibility

      We actively fulfil our corporate social responsibilities, aiming to build a shared future for our company, society, and the
      environment. In collaboration with third-party financial leasing companies, we have introduced leasing solutions for new
      energy construction machinery products, alleviating the investment burden on downstream enterprises. Downstream
      enterprises can obtain the required equipment without incurring substantial upfront capital expenditure, significantly
      reducing cash flow pressure. This not only contributes to achieving carbon neutrality but also promotes high-quality
      development. The Group maintains close engagement with communities through active participation in community
      activities, ensuring business development strategies consider community interests and benefits. During the reporting
      period, the Group’s cumulative charitable donations reached RMB100,000, with a primary focus on the field of national
      fitness.

Appendix 5 HKEX ESG Reporting Code Content Index

      INDEPENDENT AUDITOR’S REPORT




      Independent auditor’s report to the shareholders of Breton Technology Co., Ltd.
      (Incorporated in the People’s Republic of China with limited liability)


      OPINION

      We have audited the consolidated financial statements of Breton Technology Co., Ltd. (the “Company”) and its subsidiaries
      (the “Group”) set out on pages 159 to 235, which comprise the consolidated statement of financial position as at 31
      December 2025, the consolidated statement of profit or loss, the consolidated statement of profit or loss and other
      comprehensive income, the consolidated statement of changes in equity and the consolidated cash flow statement for the
      year then ended and notes, comprising material accounting policy information and other explanatory information.

      In our opinion, the consolidated financial statements give a true and fair view of the consolidated financial position of the
      Group as at 31 December 2025 and of its consolidated financial performance and its consolidated cash flows for the year
      then ended in accordance with IFRS Accounting Standards as issued by the International Accounting Standards Board (the
      “IASB”) and have been properly prepared in compliance with the disclosure requirements of the Hong Kong Companies
      Ordinance.

      BASIS FOR OPINION

      We conducted our audit in accordance with Hong Kong Standards on Auditing (“HKSAs”) as issued by the HKICPA. Our
      responsibilities under those standards are further described in the Auditor’s responsibilities for the audit of the consolidated
      financial statements section of our report. We are independent of the Group in accordance with the HKICPA’s Code of
      Ethics for Professional Accountants (the “Code”), as applicable to audits of financial statements of public interest entities.
      We have also fulfilled our other ethical responsibilities in accordance with the Code. We believe that the audit evidence we
      have obtained is sufficient and appropriate to provide a basis for our opinion.
                                                                  2025 ANNUAL REPORT       BRETON TECHNOLOGY CO., LTD.     153




INDEPENDENT AUDITOR’S REPORT



KEY AUDIT MATTERS

Key audit matters are those matters that, in our professional judgement, were of most significance in our audit of the
consolidated financial statements of the current period. These matters were addressed in the context of our audit of the
consolidated financial statements as a whole, and in forming our opinion thereon, and we do not provide a separate
opinion on these matters.


 Timing of revenue recognition on sales of new energy engineering machinery

 Refer to Note 4 to the consolidated financial statements and the accounting policies on page 181 to 182.

 The Key Audit Matter                                         How the matter was addressed in our audit

 For the year ended 31 December 2025, revenue from            Our audit procedures to assess the recognition of revenue
 sales of Battery-electric vehicles and spare parts and       on sales of new energy engineering machinery included

      INFORMATION OTHER THAN THE CONSOLIDATED FINANCIAL STATEMENTS
      AND AUDITOR’S REPORT THEREON

      The directors are responsible for the other information. The other information comprises all the information included in the
      annual report, other than the consolidated financial statements and our auditor’s report thereon.

      Our opinion on the consolidated financial statements does not cover the other information and we do not express any form
      of assurance conclusion thereon.

      In connection with our audit of the consolidated financial statements, our responsibility is to read the other information and,
      in doing so, consider whether the other information is materially inconsistent with the consolidated financial statements or
      our knowledge obtained in the audit or otherwise appears to be materially misstated.

      If, based on the work we have performed, we conclude that there is a material misstatement of this other information, we
      are required to report that fact. We have nothing to report in this regard.

      RESPONSIBILITIES OF THE DIRECTORS FOR THE CONSOLIDATED
      FINANCIAL STATEMENTS

      The directors are responsible for the preparation of the consolidated financial statements that give a true and fair view in
      accordance with IFRS Accounting Standards as issued by the IASB and the disclosure requirements of the Hong Kong
      Companies Ordinance and for such internal control as the directors determine is necessary to enable the preparation of
      consolidated financial statements that are free from material misstatement, whether due to fraud or error.

      In preparing the consolidated financial statements, the directors are responsible for assessing the Group’s ability to
      continue as a going concern, disclosing, as applicable, matters related to going concern and using the going concern
      basis of accounting unless the directors either intend to liquidate the Group or to cease operations, or have no realistic
      alternative but to do so.

      The directors are assisted by the audit committee of the Company (the “Audit Committee”) in discharging their
      responsibilities for overseeing the Group’s financial reporting process.

AUDITOR’S RESPONSIBILITIES FOR THE AUDIT OF THE CONSOLIDATED
FINANCIAL STATEMENTS

Our objectives are to obtain reasonable assurance about whether the consolidated financial statements as a whole are free
from material misstatement, whether due to fraud or error, and to issue an auditor’s report that includes our opinion. This
report is made solely to you, as a body, and for no other purpose. We do not assume responsibility towards or accept
liability to any other person for the contents of this report.

Reasonable assurance is a high level of assurance but is not a guarantee that an audit conducted in accordance with
HKSAs will always detect a material misstatement when it exists. Misstatements can arise from fraud or error and are
considered material if, individually or in the aggregate, they could reasonably be expected to influence the economic
decisions of users taken on the basis of these consolidated financial statements.

As part of an audit in accordance with HKSAs, we exercise professional judgement and maintain professional scepticism
throughout the audit. We also:

•      Identify and assess the risks of material misstatement of the consolidated financial statements, whether due to fraud
       or error, design and perform audit procedures responsive to those risks, and obtain audit evidence that is sufficient
       and appropriate to provide a basis for our opinion. The risk of not detecting a material misstatement resulting
       from fraud is higher than for one resulting from error, as fraud may involve collusion, forgery, intentional omissions,
       misrepresentations, or the override of internal control.

•      Obtain an understanding of internal control relevant to the audit in order to design audit procedures that are
       appropriate in the circumstances but not for the purpose of expressing an opinion on the effectiveness of the
       Group’s internal control.

•      Evaluate the appropriateness of accounting policies used and the reasonableness of accounting estimates and
       related disclosures made by the directors.

•      Conclude on the appropriateness of the directors’ use of the going concern basis of accounting and, based on
       the audit evidence obtained, whether a material uncertainty exists related to events or conditions that may cast
       significant doubt on the Group’s ability to continue as a going concern. If we conclude that a material uncertainty
       exists, we are required to draw attention in our auditor’s report to the related disclosures in the consolidated
       financial statements or, if such disclosures are inadequate, to modify our opinion. Our conclusions are based on the
       audit evidence obtained up to the date of our auditor’s report. However, future events or conditions may cause the
       Group to cease to continue as a going concern.

•      Evaluate the overall presentation, structure and content of the consolidated financial statements, including the
       disclosures, and whether the consolidated financial statements represent the underlying transactions and events in
       a manner that achieves fair presentation.
